Checked 2026-10-22, all runs muted:

- Compare mode, the three test cities with the camera workout (the shots harness): 0 divergences. Making Money 77,915 overlays added and removed (BucketInsert, Invalidate and RemoveOverlay each), 111,456 SetOverlayAlpha, 82,676 SetOverlayEnabled, 86 each of UpdateDecalInfo, DecalInfo, SetOverlayColor, SetOverlayDrawMode and CellRect; Big City 378,293 added, 378,925 removed, 395,314 SetOverlayEnabled, 173,906 SetOverlayAlpha, 11,846 UpdateDecalInfo, 854 of the others; Rush Hour 27,923 added, 27,929 removed, 208,312 SetOverlayAlpha, 29,800 SetOverlayEnabled, 12,116 UpdateDecalInfo, 104 of the others. SetCells, BucketRemove and FreeHandle ran inside those calls (each side its own). Two bugs put in on purpose (alphas halved, bucket nodes appended instead of pushed) gave 129 and 2,966 divergences in one view of Making Money, so the comparison sees them.
- Compare mode, Making Money for four days with `OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS=1`, `OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS_EDIT=1` (a decal, a label and a sign made, shown, faded, moved and removed) and `OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS=2`: 0 divergences, all 26 reached (UpdateDecalInfo 3,742, MoveDecal 350, the getters 1,183 each, Place and BucketRemove 2, SetHeightMapParams, GetStatsString and the message 4, QueryInterface 8).
- Screenshots with ours on, against the stock DirectX 7 baselines: Making Money 20 of 20, Rush Hour 20 of 20, Big City 19 of 20. The odd one (z1-r3, 1.34% of the pixels against a limit of 1.2%) fails the same way with the game's code (1.36%), and ours against that run is 20 of 20. Again on the final build (rebased onto main): 60 of 60.
- Parity (`renderer.overlays.manage1=ours` on side B, the base list with `determinism.dispatch_info`, 7 days, three jobs, every save on its first pair): plain 12 of 12; with `OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS=2` and `OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS=2` 12 of 12; with the propkinds edit (`OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS_EDIT=1`) and `OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS=3` 12 of 12; the same on `saves-plopped` 3 of 3. Every B run of the test legs logs a first call of 24 of the 26 (the plain legs: 11), the ` ovl` token is the same on both sides (373 to 5,784 overlays, 96 to 232 handles a city), and every edit run logs `restored yes`. Again on the final build: plain 12 of 12 and the edit leg 12 of 12, first pair; compare mode with the edit test 0 divergences in 35,047 calls, all 26 reached. Result folders `%LOCALAPPDATA%\opensc4\parity\ov1-*`.

No city in the corpus has a handle whose target is a group (the test counts 0); part 2's test makes one (a ring decal), and its runs reach and compare the group branches of the setters, the getters, MoveDecal, UpdateDecalInfo and RemoveOverlay (below). In ours mode FreeHandle and DecalMoved are called by ours directly and log no first call; the compare runs reached them. The U-Drive marker is not this class's: it goes to the situation code's overlay manager (`SC4OverlayManager`, 0x0046xxxx), and its draw's second slot is the shared stub 0x00735290.

**Part 2** (`renderer.overlays.manage2`, [overlay_manage_2.cpp](../src/core/overlay_manage_2.cpp) for ours and [overlay_manage_2_hooks.cpp](../src/core/overlay_manage_2_hooks.cpp) for the detours and compare mode, 31 functions, 15,419 bytes, translated by hand from the listing): the adding. The interface's slots 3 to 11 and what is under them: the two decal adds 0x0073AF80 and 0x0073B8A0, the new records from the free lists or the vectors' ends, the vectors' growth (STLport's insert overflow) with the record copies, fills and assigns and the empty record, the shadow's cells 0x007354E0 and the two decal builders, BuildDecal 0x0073A1B0 and the ring's 0x007394B0. Part 1's MoveDecal and UpdateDecalInfo call BuildDecal by address, so they reach ours when manage2 is on and the game's when it is off. The two parts share which side a compared call is on ([overlay_manage.h](../src/core/overlay_manage.h)), so a call one part compares reaches the other part's functions on the same side.

What the slots do. The decals: slot 3 from a texture source (an object with references and a size, its slot 0xC), slot 4 from a texture, slots 5 and 6 from the texture the texture manager (+0x90) finds by key (its slot 0x18) or by ID (0x14), through slot 4: a decal is an overlay with flags 0x90, built from a handle's description (the position, size and turn given, unit aspect and texture scales, no offset, the texture's height over its width at +0x28) without buckets, then put into its buckets and redrawn, and a handle for it. Slot 7, a ring decal: eight overlays (flags 0x10), a group of them and the handle's target the group; built on the group, each piece put into its buckets and redrawn (the terrain's slot 0x110 called inline). Slot 8, a height map over the whole terrain (flags 0x12). Slots 9 and 10, a shadow decal (flags 8) by key or by ID (slot 10 builds the key {FSH, the texture manager's group, ID} and calls slot 9): mirrored when asked, each row of the first matrix gets its fourth value less its second in place of the second; its second matrix is the first halved in its first two columns with a quarter of the fourth added; placed with part 1's Place. Slot 11, a shadow (flags 8): the lighting object (0x00B43DD0's slot 0x24) projects the points into the first matrix (its slot 0x44, nothing when that fails) and, at five texture stages or more (the driver's +0x1FC), gives the cells (0x48); below five the cells come from the bounds, 0.07 in from each side and stretched by the height away from the light (0x34, then 0x007354E0). The second matrix maps the ground points' bounds to the middle half of the texture. The shadow adds give -1 while the manager has no shadow stages (+0x18). BuildDecal: the texture matrix that maps the decal's square to its texture (turned about the centre by the turn, in turns), given to the overlay or to each of a group's eight; the cells from the decal's bounds (grown to hold a turned decal when its texture scales are 1) through CellRect, then through SetCells with buckets or cut to the terrain in place; for a group the ring's cells (outer radius half the width, the inner 0.9 of it; rows 0.2 and 0.4 of half the depth in from each end, 0x00B0D4D4 and 0x00B0D4D0; a band across the top and the bottom, the middle rows' sides, four pieces between). Flag 2 is set unless both texture scales are 1.

Some details the translation keeps: a turned decal's cosine and sine are the x87's own fcos and fsin of the angle, each rounded once to a float (their results do not follow the precision control); the decal and shadow matrices keep the game's products by zero (its matrix code is generic) and its sums in its order, with what the game keeps on the register stack at double precision and what it stores rounded, so a value is sometimes used unrounded after it was stored (the decal's fourth-row offset, the ring's last inner half-width, the shadow's inverse spans); a ring's square distances are compared before they are rounded and used rounded; the ratio of two sizes is of their unsigned values; the record copies never write the byte at +9 (a new record has what the memory held); a removed overlay keeps its texture's reference until its record is used again (part 1); the growth makes room for as many more as there are, or for the inserted count when larger. The game only ever inserts one record at a vector's end, so FillOverlays, FillHandles (an insert of more than one) and CopyGroups (the records after the insert point) run only from the test; Clear (0x0073AC90, part 3) calls the two range assigns with empty ranges.

Compare mode runs ours on a copy of the manager, then the game's on the manager itself, and compares the manager's fields, the vectors' sizes and capacities, the records the call can reach (the free records an add may take, the last free one, every record past the old end; a vector that may grow, whole), every bucket list, the terrain redraws and the result. The copy holds its own references to the textures and texture sources of the overlay records it copies, so what ours assigns and releases there leaves the game's counts alone; a vector that may grow is copied into a block from the game's allocator, which ours may free. An overlay's byte at +9 is left out. The range helpers called at the top (Clear's assigns, the test's direct calls) are compared on a scratch copy of their destination, ours' overlay copies destroyed after.

`OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS` (token ` ovl`) now also drives part 2 on each manager after part 1: slots 3 to 11 once each with the shadow texture (0xAA592B77, the one Init loads) at the terrain's middle (slot 3 with a stand-in texture source; slot 11 twice, the second time with the stage count set to four for the call, so 0x007354E0 runs), SetHeightMapParams on the height map, a decal moved east and back (BuildDecal with buckets); the ring decal's handle through part 1's getters, setters, DecalInfo, MoveDecal and two UpdateDecalInfo (turned with a texture scale of 2, then its own), which is the group path of all of them and BuildDecal's into SetCells; on the first manager with buckets the range helpers by address on scratch records and ring decals until all three vectors have grown. Then everything added is removed again, last first (part 1's RemoveOverlay, the ring's through its group branch): the overlays in use are as they were, the vectors larger and their free lists longer. The token gains the count of added IDs and of vectors grown.

Checked 2026-10-22, all runs muted:

- Compare mode (manage1 ours, so part 1's MoveDecal and UpdateDecalInfo reach BuildDecal at the top), the three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences. Making Money 78,687 AddShadow, 86 AddDecal by ID and 86 BuildDecal; Big City 379,837 AddShadow, 945,050 BuildDecal, 1,046 AddDecal by ID; Rush Hour 27,837 AddShadow, 309,206 BuildDecal, 104 AddDecal by ID; the two range assigns 4 each (Clear). 17 of the 31 reached in each (the rest only by the test).
- Compare mode, Making Money for four days with `OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS=1`, `OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS_EDIT=1` and `OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS=2`, with manage1 compared too: 0 divergences, all 31 of part 2 reached (AddShadow 959, AddDecal by ID 100, from a texture source 30, by key 5, from a texture, the height map and both shadow decals 4 each, the ring decal 12, the range assigns 5, the fills and copies 1 each; BuildDecal inside part 1's compared calls) and all 26 of part 1, whose compared calls now include a target that is a group (RemoveOverlay 1,122, the setters and getters, MoveDecal 302, UpdateDecalInfo 1,374); `restored yes`; 48 IDs added and removed, three vectors grown. Again with manage1 ours: 0 divergences, BuildDecal 15,832, all 31 reached. A bug put in on purpose (the shadow's v shift from the rounded inverse span) gave 9 divergences in 951 AddShadow calls in two days of Making Money, so the comparison sees a difference in the last bit.
- Screenshots with manage1 and manage2 ours, against the stock DirectX 7 baselines: Making Money 20 of 20, Big City 20 of 20 (z1-r3 too this time), Rush Hour 20 of 20.
- Parity (`renderer.overlays.manage1=ours,renderer.overlays.manage2=ours` on side B, the base list with `determinism.dispatch_info`, 7 days, three jobs, every save on its first pair): plain 12 of 12; with `OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS=2` and `OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS=2` 12 of 12; with the propkinds edit (`OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS_EDIT=1`) and `OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS=3` 12 of 12; the same on `saves-plopped` 3 of 3. Every B run of the test legs logs a first call of 17 of the 31 (the others are called by ours directly), the ` ovl` token is the same on both sides (373 to 5,784 overlays, 48 to 365 IDs added and removed a city, all three vectors grown in each), and every edit run logs `restored yes`. Result folders `%LOCALAPPDATA%\opensc4\parity\ov2-*`.

Not reached by the game in any run: FillOverlays, FillHandles and CopyGroups (no caller inserts more than one record or away from the end) and 0x007354E0 (every driver in the tests reports five stages or more); the test reaches them, and only its calls compare them. Slot 11's transform in the test is made up (identity rotation at the terrain's middle, scale 1); the lighting object reads it, the comparison does not depend on what it means. In ours mode the functions under the slots are called by ours directly and log no first call; the compare runs reached them.

**Part 3** (`renderer.overlays.manage3`, [overlay_manage_3.cpp](../src/core/overlay_manage_3.cpp) for ours and [overlay_manage_3_hooks.cpp](../src/core/overlay_manage_3_hooks.cpp) for the detours and compare mode, 23 functions, 3,103 bytes, translated by hand from the listing): the set-up and the containers. The constructor, Init, Clear, the destructor's body and the deleting destructor, the five render states and their vector, the bucket grid (one bucket a cell) and the buckets' vector of list heads (resized, n lists inserted, a new block), STLport's slist helpers for its lists (assigned, cut after a node, nodes copied, ranges of lists made, assigned forwards and backwards, emptied, destroyed), the overlay records destroyed over a range and the view interface's draw. Part 2's GrowOverlays and the autotest call the records' destructor 0x00737E10 by address, so they reach ours when manage3 is on; Clear calls part 2's two range assigns by address. Calls among the 23 are ours directly.

What they do. The constructor sets the bases' vtables, cRZBaseUnknown at +8 (reference count 0), then the class's four; the shadow stages 2, +0x20 1, three state blocks of twelve bytes at +0x38, the terrain's size unset (0x00B4B3C8), every vector empty and every free list -1; the cells, the clip, +0x94 and the bucket grid's fields keep what the memory held. The terrain view AddRefs each manager after making it, then calls Init (the view, whether the manager draws the water's rows, the byte at +0x16; the third and fourth managers draw them and get no buckets): the view, the texture manager (the framework's 0x1AC0E11A) and the terrain (0x00B43CF4) held; the terrain's cells, a cell's size and its inverse, the terrain's size (the cells stored as floats times the size the terrain's slot 0x18 leaves on the x87's stack, each product rounded once to a float), the clip; the shadow texture 0xAA592B77; five render states (0x007D2B50); the buckets; the options message wanted from the message server, which AddRefs the target (the manager's count is then 2); the stage count and the detail from the driver. Clear (the terrain view's stop) unwants the message (the server's Release), releases the view, the terrain and the texture manager, destroys every overlay record (their textures and sources released) and empties the overlays' and handles' vectors and free lists, and clears +0x14; it leaves the groups and the buckets, which the destructor frees. The destructor's body releases the shadow texture, frees every block and destroys the bucket lists and the records, and clears nothing; the deleting destructor (reached through the adjustor thunk 0x0073BDC0 on the last Release) then frees the memory.

Some details the translation keeps: the buckets' vector grows to as many more lists as there are or to the inserted count when larger, so Init's first insert makes it exactly one list a cell, and the first insert after that doubles it; an insert with room copies the list to a local first (it may be one of the vector's), but one that needs a new block takes it as it is; a shrink assigns the lists past the new end from an empty range (a call that does nothing) before destroying them; an insert's lists past the place are moved by assigning them, which reuses their nodes; a node's value is written only when the node's second word is not at address 0 (the placement new's test); ResizeStates and the bucket helpers count with the byte difference shifted right arithmetically and compare unsigned; Init reads the driver's global twice.

Compare mode. The game calls five of the 23 from outside the class: the constructor, Init, Clear, the deleting destructor and the view draw. The constructor runs ours on a copy of the memory as it was and compares every byte. Init and Clear run ours on a copy of the manager that holds its own references (the view, the terrain, the texture manager, and for Clear the overlay records' textures and sources), with the message server's global pointing at a stand-in for the call: its slots 0x14 and 0x18 for the manager or the copy are recorded on both sides (the game's then passed on, ours not), every other call is passed on to the real server. The fields are compared (the vectors by size and capacity, the reference count at +0xC left out: the server's AddRef and Release change it on the game's side only), with Init's render states and every bucket list, the recorded calls, and for Clear the textures' reference counts after ours (each back where it was before the copy took its own). After Init the copy is destroyed by the game's destructor. The deleting destructor runs ours's body on a deep copy (its blocks from the game's allocator, its nodes and references its own), then the game's deleting destructor, whose call to the body is compared before the game frees the memory: the fields left and the textures' counts. The view draw only calls Draw, which draws, so compare mode runs ours (counted, not compared). The containers are reached only from inside those calls, each side its own, except the autotest's calls by address: a list assigned, n lists inserted, the buckets' vector resized (compared on copies of the list or the vector: the values, the sizes, whether a new block was taken, and in place which of the old nodes each place reuses) and the records destroyed (compared by the textures' and sources' reference counts).

`OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS` (token ` ovl`) now also drives part 3 after part 2, on a scratch manager made with operator new on the first manager with buckets: constructed, AddRef'd as the terrain view does, set up with that manager's view; its buckets' lists assigned from longer, shorter and equal lists and from themselves (the cut and the copy of nodes); the vector shrunk by ten, lists inserted in place with more after them than inserted (the copy backwards), with fewer, from one of its own, then one at full capacity (a new block, one list, the tail copied) and four at the end; then shrunk back; a decal added (part 2's slot 4), Clear and the last Release. The fields that do not depend on the memory, the render states and every list are hashed. The token gains no field (the hash covers it); the log line counts the scratch managers.

Checked 2026-10-22, all runs muted:

- An independent audit of the 23 against the listing (every call's argument count against the callee's `ret`, the fields, the compares): no defects.
- Compare mode (manage1 and manage2 ours, so part 2's GrowOverlays reaches the records' destructor at the top), the three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences. Each city: the constructor, Init, Clear and the deleting destructor 4 each (compared), the records destroyed 22, 38 and 24 times at the top (Making Money, Big City, Rush Hour), the view draw 109,222, 106,990 and 113,790; inside the compared calls the render states and the bucket grid (2 managers with buckets), GrowBuckets 2, CopyNodes 32,768, FillNLists 2, CopyNewLists 4, DestroyLists 10. 17 of the 23 reached in each; the game never assigns, cuts, copies forwards or backwards or empties a single bucket list (it only makes the empty grid and destroys it).
- Compare mode, Making Money for four days with `OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS=1`, `OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS_EDIT=1` and `OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS=2`, with manage1, manage2 and manage3 compared: 0 divergences in all three parts, all 23 of part 3 reached (the constructor, Init, Clear and the destructor 5 each, AssignList 6 at the top and 32,756 inside, InsertLists 4, ResizeBuckets 3, the records destroyed 2 at the top; CutAfter 32,743, CopyNodes 65,567, CopyListsBackward 2, ClearList 4); `restored yes`. Again with manage1 and manage2 ours: 0 divergences, all 23 reached, the same ` ovl` hash. A bug put in on purpose (the destructor not releasing the shadow texture) gave 4 divergences in 4 destructor comparisons in two days of Making Money.
- Screenshots with manage1, manage2 and manage3 ours, against the stock DirectX 7 baselines: Making Money 20 of 20, Rush Hour 20 of 20, Big City 19 of 20. The odd one is z1-r3 again (1.93%); with part 3 the game's it fails the same way (1.61%), with all three ours again 1.71%, and ours against the run with the game's part 3 is 20 of 20.
- Parity (`renderer.overlays.manage1=ours,renderer.overlays.manage2=ours,renderer.overlays.manage3=ours` on side B, the base list with `determinism.dispatch_info`, 7 days, three jobs, every save on its first pair): plain 12 of 12; with `OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS=2` and `OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS=2` 12 of 12; with the propkinds edit (`OPENSC4_TEST_PROPKINDS_EDIT=1`) and `OPENSC4_TEST_OVERLAYS=3` 12 of 12; the same on `saves-plopped` 3 of 3. The ` ovl` token is the same on both sides in all 27 pairs of the test legs, and every edit run logs `restored yes`. B runs log a first call of 9 of the 23 in the test legs and 6 in the plain legs (the others are called by ours directly). Result folders `%LOCALAPPDATA%\opensc4\parity\ov3-*`.

The overlay manager is done: every function of the class in the census's range is ours (the draws and parts 1 to 3) except the shared stub 0x00735290 and the six adjustor thunks.

Next: the effects drawing and the automata drawing surveys.

## Models and lots

**How the 3D render draws a frame** (`cSC43DRender::Draw`, 0x007CB530; the class is 158 functions, about 40 KB, 0x007C2000 to 0x007CC000, vtable 0x00ABAB60): a pending scroll first (0x007CABE0), then the dirty rectangles (0x007C9600), the context's frame start (0x007D5230), the camera view's update (0x007F9F50 on the object at +0x114), the background (`ClearViewport`, slot 0xBC, 0x007C3F80, which despite its name restores or redraws what lies under the objects), then the passes: the static pass (0x007C7370, when the preference at +0x5C is set), a save of the picture (0x007C2960), the moving-objects pass (0x007C7830, preference +0x3C) and a last one (0x007C86D0, preference +0x2DC). The things drawn are drawables with a virtual draw (slot 0x30, given the render context), kept in a grid (+0x120) by position.

**The static pass** (0x007C7370) takes the bounds of the view on the ground (0x007C1FD0) and the cells of the grid they cover (0x005F29C0), and from each cell the items with flag 8 (+6), each once: in its own first cell, or in the first cell of the range. Each gets a key: its depth against a plane from the camera view (0x007C2F80, 0x007C2EB0, from the item's bounding box) plus the drawable's own order (its slot 0x44) in the high half. The {drawable, key} pairs are sorted (0x007C56D0) and drawn in rounds: a drawable that answers 2 is drawn again in the next round, the pairs going back and forth between two vectors (+0x134, +0x140), up to 12 rounds, with the round number in the context (+4).

`renderer.models.static` replaces the pass (the helpers above and the drawables' own drawing still the game's). Compared draw by draw in the same frame. The first comparison had the right number of draws everywhere but about one frame per view with a draw that differed, and the game's pass against itself did the same: drawables change on their first draw (a texture arrives and a placeholder goes, the tree pop-in of the first seconds). The comparison for this pass therefore runs the game's once quietly first, so both compared runs see the drawables settled. 2026-10-10 with that, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (1,242, 1,439 and 663 passes; the game against itself also 0); 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver.

**The moving-objects pass** (0x007C7830) walks the same cells but only those whose flag word (+0xC, bit 0) says they hold what it wants: items without flag 8, or with flag 0x80. It rewrites each cell's flag as it goes, so a cell stays flagged only while it holds such an item. Items without flag 8 go to one of three lists by their flags (1: +0x14C; 0x100: +0x140; neither: +0x134, with the drawable's order in the key as in the static pass); items with flag 0x80 also go to a fourth (+0x128). The three lists are sorted (the first by 0x007C56D0, the others by 0x007C5710) and drawn in rounds, each list compacted in place to the drawables that answered 2; the round number runs on across the lists and ends the pass at 12. The fourth list is drawn last, each with a second argument of 1 and a depth offset of 4.

`renderer.models.moving` replaces it, compared as the static pass (a quiet run of the game's first). 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (47,927, 39,306 and 56,602 passes; 35,284 on Big City with cars, clouds and water effects on; the game against itself also 0); 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver.

**What the passes draw.** A count by the drawable's vtable over a Big City shots run with cars, clouds and water effects on (`OPENSC4_MODELS_CENSUS=1`, 4.4 million draws; each vtable's slot 0x30, most through an adjustor for a virtual base):

| Vtable | Draw function | Share | What |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0x00ABD1D0 | 0x007FEA90 | 41% | a camera-facing textured quad (billboard) |
| 0x00ABD508, 0x00ABD018 | 0x00801750 | 30% | a model, through the context's 0x007D5710 |
| 0x00AA2C30 | 0x005E0200 | 20% | a textured quad on the ground |
| 0x00AA7F10 | 0x0061A850 | 4% | a quad as one or two triangles, and a list of triangles |
| 0x00AA2EA8 | 0x005E27C0 | 2% | a strip of triangles shown from two rotations |
| 0x00AA2808 | 0x005D5FC0 | 2% | quads picked by the rotation |

**The billboard** (0x007FEA90; `this` is the object + 0x170) draws nothing for the overlaid round or below zoom 2. It keeps the zoom and rotation its picture was picked for (+0x88, +0x89); when they change (or +0x9C is set) it picks again from a heading (the x87 arctangent of its direction's +0x5C over +0x44) and a pitch (the arcsine of +0x50) through 0x007FE390, and after a change through 0x007FE400. Its four corners (+0x13C) plus its position (+0x60) make the four vertices (+0xDC, 24 bytes each) when that or a state byte (+0x38) changed. Then the colour (one of two ways by flag 4 of +0x20), the render state (+0x8C), with flag 2 an alpha (+0x30), the texture for the picture (+0xC4 by the index at +0x94) and a four-vertex draw.

`renderer.models.billboard` replaces it (the two helpers still the game's; the arctangent is the x87 instruction and the arcsine the game's own C runtime, so the results are the same to the bit). Compared draw by draw per billboard. 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (3,098, 797,844 and 36,697 billboards; the game against itself also 0); 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver with all 17 renderer replacements on.

**The model draw** (the render context's 0x007D5710: an instance, a model, properties, the overlaid flag; the two model drawables' slot 0x30 goes straight to it, 0x00801750). Properties are a flags byte (4: plain colour; 8: not in the overlaid round) and a colour at +4. With the preference at +0x3DC set, a model whose bounding box (the instance's slot 0x20) measures less on screen than a threshold is left out. The instance fills a transform (its slot 0x24: a 3x3 rotation, a position and a scale), which the context takes (0x007D3400); the colour is set through the lighting (0x007D31E0) or, for plain colour or the overlaid round, as the driver's colour multiplier. Then each of the model's groups (count from its slot 0xC): the material (slot 0x18) is set up (0x007D5310, or 0x007D5670 overlaid) and the mesh (slot 0x10) drawn (0x007D4A80); without a second texture stage a material of kind 2 (+0xB) gets a second pass (0x007D47E0). Last the colour multiplier is put back and the alpha multiplier set to 1. A debug preference (+0x35C) draws bounding boxes instead; that path stays the game's.

`renderer.models.model` replaces it (material setup and mesh drawing still the game's). Compared per model, with a quiet run of the game's first as for the passes. 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (306,555, 852,057 and 227,232 models; the game against itself also 0); 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver with all 18 renderer replacements on.

**The mesh draw** (the context's 0x007D4A80). A mesh is three pointers: a vertex buffer (the format, at +6 a count that must not be 0, at +8 the vertices), an index buffer or null, and its primitive groups (a vector at +4 of {primitive, first, count}). The vertices go to the driver once (`InterleavedArrays`), then each group is a `DrawElements` with 16-bit indices from the index buffer at `first`, or a `DrawArrays` when there is none. `renderer.models.mesh` replaces it. 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (592,600, 902,582 and 366,084 meshes); 20 of 20 screenshots.

**The material setup** (the context's 0x007D5310: a material and the properties). A material has 16 flag bits at +4, state values at +6 to +0xA, an alpha (0 to 255) at +8, the number of texture stages (0 to 2) at +0xB, then 0x14 bytes per stage from +0x10: the texture (by two pointers), wrap (+4, +5), filters (+6, +7), the texture coordinate source (+8), the environment mode (+9), a flag for a moving texture (+0xA) and its period (+0xC). The setup packs a render state from them (0x007D2B50, fourteen values) and applies it (0x007D4530); the properties' flags bend it: 8 dims the colour by the value at +0x10 (the driver's colour multiplier), 2 makes the model translucent by that value (blend values 4 and 5, the alpha scaled, the driver's alpha multiplier), 0x10 and 0x20 pick other values for three of the fields. Then, when the material's flag 0x20 is set, each stage: the texture and environment mode straight to the driver past the context's stage cache (the second stage only with the second-stage preference), wrap, filters and coordinate source through the context, and for a moving texture a texture matrix that scales by 1 / period and shifts by the context's two values at +0x19C and +0x1A0. `renderer.models.material` replaces it; compare mode compares the driver's state and the context after ours and after the game's. 2026-10-10: 0 divergences (1,004,240, 1,031,155 and 651,468 materials); 20 of 20 screenshots with all 21 renderer replacements on.

The overlaid round's material setup (0x007D5670: one stage, straight to the driver) is written as `renderer.models.material_overlaid` but no model in the test cities is drawn in that round, so it is unverified.

**The ground quad** (0x005E0200 after the adjustor 0x005E0540; `this` is the object + 0xEC) draws nothing in the overlaid round. It follows the context's zoom (0x005DFD40 when it differs from +0x45) and rotation (its own slot 0x10, then +0x46). It has three ways to draw: tinted when its tint mode (+0x40) is not 0xFF, translucent when a flag (+0x44) is set or its alpha (+0x3F) is below 255, else plain; the first two answer 2 in the first round, so they are drawn in the second, after everything opaque. Each way has a fixed render state (0x00B23090, 0x00B23098, 0x00B230A0) with the quad's texture (+0x48 by the index at +0x41). Tinted, the colour (+0x3C, scaled by a light value from the context's +0x44 object and by 1/255) and alpha go to the context as its light vector, with the tint mode; a preference (+0x3BC) turns mode 3 into mode 1 at half the alpha. The quad is then drawn from its own four vertices (+0x64) when it has them (+0xC4), else through the context's +0x4C object (slot 0x3C), which lays it on the terrain.

`renderer.models.groundquad` replaces it (the zoom change and the draw on the terrain still the game's), compared per quad with a quiet run of the game's first. 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (19,948, 601,829 and 17,829 quads; the game against itself also 0); 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver with all 22 renderer replacements on.

**Two rarer drawables.** The one with vtable 0x00AA2EA8 (0x005E27C0; `this` is the object + 0xAC) follows the zoom and rotation through two helpers (0x005E1710, 0x005E1BC0), then, when it is visible (+0x66) and the rotation is one of the two its side (+0x78) faces, draws its vertices (+0x9C to +0xA0, 24 bytes each) as one strip with its texture (+0x98) and a fixed render state (0x00B230B4). The one with vtable 0x00AA2808 (0x005D5FC0; `this` is the object + 0x274) takes two indices from the rotation (the camera view's 0x007F7750, to +0x50 and +0x54), and draws the quads they pick (from +0x74, 0x60 bytes each) with one texture (+0x70) when the zoom's flag (+0x4A) is set, then one more quad (+0x210) with a second texture (+0x20C), all with the render state at 0x00B23034. Neither draws in the overlaid round.

`renderer.models.sidestrip` and `renderer.models.rotationquads` replace them (their zoom and rotation helpers still the game's). 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (1,103, 81,080 and 1,563 strips; 882, 66,915 and 1,693 of the quads; the game against itself also 0); 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver with all 24 renderer replacements on.

**The last drawable** (vtable 0x00AA7F10, 0x0061A850; `this` is the object + 0x17C) asks itself about its flags through its own slot 0x20 and looks textures up through its slot 0x134. It picks one of two render states (0x00B45260, or 0x00B45268 when the pieces it reports through slot 0xB4 do not combine to one of twelve plain patterns). With either of the flags 0x6000000 it draws its quad (+0x9C) with up to two textures, each as one or two triangles by the flags 0x2000000 and 0x4000000, from one of two index lists by flag 0x1000000. Then it draws a list of single triangles (+0x118, 0x4C bytes each: a texture key and three vertices), leaving out those the terrain (0x00B43CF4, slot 0x78) has risen above. Unlike the others it also draws in the overlaid round: there, after asking its second interface (slot 0x40) whether to, it uses a fixed colour and draws everything a second time with a third render state (0x00B45270).

`renderer.models.flaggedquad` replaces it. 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (5,102, 157,704 and 5,258 objects; the game against itself also 0); 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver with all 25 renderer replacements on. With it every kind of drawable the passes meet in the test cities is drawn by our code.

**The sign and the burnt building** draw themselves through their model interface (slot 12; [propkinds.md](propkinds.md)): the sign as an untextured board, its text's glyphs, the caret while edited and ten textured quads of frame and post, from geometry its builder lays out to face the camera; the burnt building as one ground quad with a texture matrix. `simulators.propkinds.sign_draw` and `simulators.propkinds.burnt_draw` replace both draws, the builders and the other slots of their own. Their compare mode compares the draws in the same frame as the passes above (a quiet run of the game's first, then ours off the screen, then the game's) and the builders' results byte for byte. 2026-10-19, Making Money with the props the propkinds test leaves, 20 views centred on them with `renderer.d3d11=ours`: 0 divergences (95 sign draws, 96 burnt building draws, 21 geometry builds); 20 of 20 screenshots against the game's with the stock driver.

**Quads laid on the terrain.** The object the render context holds at +0x4C is the terrain view's interface at its +0xC (vtable 0x00AB4410); its slot 0x3C (0x00752940: render context, x, z, eight texture coordinates, an alpha, a flag) draws one cell of the land as a quad: the four land vertices of the cell copied, their texture coordinates replaced by the caller's and their alpha by the caller's, split along the cell's diagonal (the terrain record's +0x23, bits 0x30). Without the flag it draws only cells inside the rows in view. This is how a ground quad without vertices of its own follows the terrain. `renderer.terrain.cellquad` replaces it. 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (12,359, 338,854 and 8,659 quads); 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver with all 26 renderer replacements on.

*(Translation removed 2026-10-22, rewrite pending.)* **The billboard's corners** (0x007FE400, after a change of zoom or rotation) are about 490 instructions of x87 arithmetic with some forty branches: a scale from the camera, the four corners from the picture's rectangle and the camera's axes, and a bounding box grown corner by corner. `renderer.models.billboard_corners` replaces it with a translation made by `tools/re/x86cpp.py` (removed), which turned a whole function of the game into C++ statements over an emulated machine (registers as integers, memory by address, the x87 stack as doubles, branches as gotos, calls to the game's own functions): `billboard_corners.inc` (removed). Compare mode runs ours, puts the billboard back, runs the game's and compares the billboard's bytes. 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (3, 639 and 15 calls); 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver with all 27 renderer replacements on.

*(Translation removed 2026-10-22, rewrite pending.)* **The background redraw** (`cSC43DRender::ClearViewport`, 0x007C3F80, slot 0xBC; 619 instructions) is replaced by `renderer.frame.background` with a translation by the same tool (`render_clear_viewport.inc` (removed)). The tool now works out most calls by itself (the argument bytes from the callee's `ret N` or the caller's `add esp`, a float result from what follows the call) and refuses to guess for a helper that ends in a jump to another function; two such helpers here (0x007FC2D0, the context's `DrawArrays`, and 0x007D28A0) had first been guessed wrong, which crashed the first run inside our own draw recorder. Compared draw by draw as the passes, with a quiet run of the game's first. 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (1,226, 1,915 and 661 redraws; the game against itself also 0); 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver with all 28 renderer replacements on.

*(Translation removed 2026-10-22, rewrite pending.)* **The pass runner and the changes before a frame.** `renderer.frame.draw` replaces `cSC43DRender::Draw` (0x007CB530) and `renderer.frame.changes` its handling of the list of changes (0x007C9600, with a jump table by kind of change), both as translations (`render_draw.inc` (removed), `render_changes.inc` (removed)). The draw-call trace hooks the same function, so the two share one hook (`frame_trace::SetDrawBody`). Neither can be compared in the same frame: a frame cannot be run twice, and the list of changes is used up as it is worked through. Their check is the screenshots: 2026-10-10 with all 30 renderer replacements on, 20 of 20 against the stock driver on Making Money; with the camera workout, against runs without the two, 20 of 20 on Making Money and Rush Hour and 19 of 20 on Big City, where the one shot that differs (the closest zoom) also differs between two runs without them (18 and 19 of 20 between such runs).

**Left to the game in this layer:** the screen shake of disasters (0x007C86D0, the frame's last step), which follows the real clock and does nothing while no disaster shakes the view, so neither a same-frame comparison nor the test cities can check it; how a billboard picks its picture (0x007FE390 with 0x007FD2F0 and 0x007FD3D0); the helpers named "not analysed" above (the zoom and rotation changes of the rarer drawables, the context's state setters); and the camera view's update (0x007F9F50).

## Cell marks

*Translation removed 2026-10-22: the game's own code runs again; a hand-written rewrite is pending (see "Translations removed" above). What follows describes the game's code and how the removed translation was checked.*

**Data views** were the next layer in the plan, but a survey found little drawing in them: the data map is painted by the map window's own code (`cSC4WinMapView`, 0x007A0D50 to 0x007A8700, with its picture drawn by 0x007A2F60), which is interface code, and no call was found from that window into the 3D render or the terrain. What the city view does have is a pass that marks terrain cells.

**The cell marks** (class unnamed, vtable 0x00AB42F8, code 0x007504F0 to 0x00751C40; a view object of the 3D render's pass 5; its draw is 0x007514F0) are two hash tables keyed by cell (z times the width at +0xC0, plus x). The coloured ones (+0xB0, drawn by 0x00750FC0) map a cell to a colour index: each is drawn as a quad at the cell's four terrain corners, split along the terrain's diagonal, in one of a few fixed colours with an alpha per colour, with the render state at 0x00B4BDA0. In a test the first two indices came out blue and yellow and the higher ones black, which suggests the zone colours of the zoning tools; that reading is a guess from the picture. The textured ones (+0x9C, drawn by 0x00750CF0) carry a 36-byte record with a texture and are drawn with a second pair of render states. Both are drawn with a depth offset of 4. Cells are marked through the terrain view's interface (slot 0xC, 0x0075A230, which calls 0x007516D0 with a list of cells and a colour index).

`renderer.terrain.cellmarks` replaces the draw and both passes, as translations (`cell_marks_coloured.inc` (removed), `cell_marks_textured.inc` (removed)). Nothing marks cells in an unattended run, so `OPENSC4_TEST_CELL_MARKS=1` marks 720 cells around the middle of the city in five colours through the game's own function. 2026-10-11 with that switch, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (12,199, 10,370 and 12,424 passes; the game against itself also 0); without the switch 20 of 20 screenshots against the stock driver. The textured marks are translated but nothing in the tests fills their table, so they are unverified.

## The data map

*Translation removed 2026-10-22: the game's own code runs again; a hand-written rewrite is pending (see "Translations removed" above). What follows describes the game's code and how the removed translation was checked.*

The Data Views window (class `cSC4WinMapView`, ID 0x28C5A41F; code 0x007A0D50 to 0x007A8700) shows a small picture of the city for the map picked in its list. The picture is not drawn through the driver: the window builds it on the CPU in a `cIGZBuffer` (class 0xC470D325, 32 bits per pixel) the size of its picture control (child 0x4203, 256 by 256 in the tests) and hands the buffer to the control, which keeps it at +0x1C. The corner minimap is something else.

- **Set map** (0x007A49B0, `SetMap(map)`): 0 for none, else the data view 0x4A0B6800 + map (kept at +0x9CC). It sets up the city view's occupant filter for the map and paints. It refuses while +0x142 is 0, which it is until the window has been shown.
- **The picture** (0x007A2F60, 6.4 KB): finds the control, makes the buffer, clears it, then switches on the map type at +0x144 (not the list's number: map 3 gives type 4, map 5 type 6). Each case asks a simulator for its map and gives it to a painter. Afterwards it walks the list of the map's icons and blits them, hands the buffer over and stamps the time at +0x9DC. A counter at 0x00B4E1A8 steps from 0 to 15, one step per picture, for the maps that move.
- **The painters** (0x007A2080 to 0x007A2F5D): a simulator's map comes as a vector of rows. The row painter (0x007A2380) takes byte rows, resamples them to the city's size when +0x94C is set (calling itself with the result), smooths them when +0x94D is set, and paints through the window's colour table at +0x54C. Three more turn rows of unsigned 16-bit, signed 16-bit and float cells into byte rows and call it; a twin (0x007A2740) does the first map type with loops of its own; and 0x007A2080 fills a 16-bit grid at +0x9B8 from two of a simulator's maps.
- **Repaints**: the window's message handler (0x007A54D0, `this` is the window + 4) repaints on message 0x86AD10EE when the picture is marked changed (+0x9D5) and the last one is 1000 ms old (250 ms for map type 0x4C).

`renderer.datamap.picture` is the picture function and `renderer.datamap.painters` the six painters, both translated instruction by instruction with `tools/re/x86cpp.py` (removed). Their calls (222 in the picture function alone, most of them virtual) go through one helper, `DynCall`: it runs the callee on a copy of the top of the emulated stack with the emulated registers loaded, and reads back the registers, how much the callee took off the stack and whether it left a float on the x87 stack. So nothing has to be known about a callee, and register arguments (the C runtime's 64-bit shift) work without a special case.

`renderer.datamap.pixels` is the nine routines under the painters, translated the same way: for each of the two row painters a resampler to the city's size (0x0079EB40, 0x0079EFD0), the pixel loop that gives every buffer pixel its cell's colour from the table (0x0079ED90, 0x0079F230), a smoother (0x0079FEF0, 0x007A00B0) and a collector of the map's outlines for the city view (0x007A1020, 0x007A1120), plus the resize of a vector of rows (0x007A1310). They are plain functions that the game's optimiser may hand arguments in registers, so their hooks save every register, give the translation the registers and the stack as the caller left them, and put EAX, ECX and EDX back as the translation leaves them. With the three replacements on, no code of the game's own is left between the simulators' maps and the finished picture except the buffer class and the containers.

`renderer.datamap.helpers` is seven more routines the picture function uses: a blended pixel, fill and ring (0x0079DC00, 0x0079E0C0, 0x0079E130), the blit of an icon into the picture, centred, clipped and turned (0x0079E600), the marking of every cell a simulator picks with one picture (0x0079F4D0), and two small coordinate setters used through pointers (0x0079DDE0, 0x0079DDF0).

Compare mode paints ours, copies the picture out of the control, puts back what a run changes that the next one reads (the flag bytes at +0x141, +0x94C and +0x9D9 and the animation step), paints the game's with the game's painters, and compares the two pictures pixel by pixel. `OPENSC4_TERRAIN_SELFCHECK=1` paints the game's twice the same way.

Checked on the three tutorial cities with every map number from 1 to 87: 44 of them are maps (the rest `SetMap` refuses), giving 33 map types (1, 2, 4, 6 to 11, 20 to 30, 64 to 76), and every picture is the game's pixel for pixel, 132 pictures in all with no difference; the self-check is clean too, and screenshots with both replacements on match the game's. The same 132 pictures have no difference with the pixel routines ours as well. Five of the six painters ran; the grid filler (0x007A2080) did not, and neither did the map types the list's numbers do not give. Six of the nine pixel routines ran; the second row painter always took its resampling path in the tests, so its own pixel loop, smoother and outline collector (0x0079F230, 0x007A00B0, 0x007A1120) did not. Those paths are written but unverified. The outline collector's lists go to the city view, not into the picture, so the pixel comparison does not see them; only the screenshots do. With the helpers ours as well the same 132 pictures have no difference; five of the seven helpers ran, the two coordinate setters did not.

To see it in a test: `OPENSC4_TEST_DATA_MAP=<n>[,<n>...]` with `diagnostics.datamap_select=ours` (not together with `ui.map.part4`, which replaces the same message handler; the two functions are `SC4UI::F7A49B0` and `SC4UI::F7A54D0` in the address database). Before the first shot the autotest sends the advisors' script command for the window (`{0x6A5BDFCD, 0}` on the queue of the object at 0x00B43DC4), which opens it; that command's number does not pick a map, so from the second shot on the pick goes to `SetMap` from inside the window's own message handler.

To reach the two coordinate setters in a test: `OPENSC4_TEST_DATA_MAP_GRID=<day>` (token `dmgrid`, [datamap_autotest.cpp](../src/core/datamap_autotest.cpp)). They are handed to two grid mappers (0x0068C270, 0x006B4710) by the grid filler, which the picture function calls only for the map types 0x40 to 0x4B with the style 5 at +0x148, a style no data view of the game has. The test picks three such maps through `SetMap`, sets the style to 5 for one more call of the picture function each and puts it back, and hooks the two mappers for those calls to count the setters' calls and take the cells they set. 2026-10-11 on Big City, 7 days: 49,152 calls of each setter (three grids of 128 by 128 cells) with the same cells from the game alone and with `renderer.datamap.picture` and `renderer.datamap.helpers` ours; with the picture in compare mode the three pictures (map types 64, 65 and 66) have no difference. The grid filler itself was the game's in these runs (`renderer.datamap.painters` was off). Choosing maps changes what the city view shows, so from its day on the occupant parts of the fingerprint differ from a run without the test, in the game alone as well.

## The mini map

*Translation removed 2026-10-22: the game's own code runs again; a hand-written rewrite is pending (see "Translations removed" above). What follows describes the game's code and how the removed translation was checked.*

The small map in the corner is a window of class `cSC4WinMiniMap` (ID 0xCA318388, 0x96C bytes; the layout is in `src/core/ui_map_6.cpp`). The same class is the Data Views window's picture control, so there are two of them once that window is open. Like the data map it is a picture built on the CPU: the object keeps its own square of pixels (+0x114, side +0xE4) and a `cIGZBuffer` (+0xF0), and an owner can hand it a second buffer (+0xF4; this is how the data map's picture gets under it).

- **Update** (0x007A8640, slot 9 of the interface at +0xD8; called when a city is loaded and once a second): does nothing while the window or a parent is hidden or +0xFE is clear. Otherwise it redoes the terrain if +0xFD is set, marks every cell that has something on it (two bits a cell at +0x960: asked, and occupied; the occupant manager is asked once a cell), and puts the picture's pixels into the buffer (0x007A66F0), or over the owner's buffer (0x007A67F0).
- **Terrain** (0x007A7FF0): redoes the 16 by 16 cell tiles marked at +0x120, taking colours from the two tables of 256 at +0x160 and +0x560 and the sixteen at +0x108, and writes rows with one of five routines picked by the picture's scale at +0x104 (0x007A6A70, 0x007A6AD0, 0x007A6BD0, 0x007A6EE0, 0x007A6E60).
- **Paint** (0x007A79B0, window slot 88): draws the buffer into the window, works out four points through the camera (0x00800340) for the part of the city in view, and toggles the blink byte at +0x102.

`renderer.minimap.draw` is those three and the nine pixel routines under them (0x007A65D0 to 0x007A707D), translated instruction by instruction and entered with every register saved, like the data map's routines. Compare mode runs every update twice: ours, then everything it touched put back (the object, its pixels, the cell bits and both buffers' pixels), then the game's with the game's own routines under it, and the two results are compared byte for byte. An update that moves the pixels or the cells (the first, which makes them) cannot be repeated and is counted apart.

Checked on the three tutorial cities, with and without the Data Views window open: 135 updates that had work to do (the rest find the picture up to date), no difference; the self-check is clean; screenshots match the game's (20 of 20 plain, 8 of 8 with the window open). The paint is compared the same way since the small windows' work (below): a window's paint draws into the window's canvas, a `cIGZBuffer` at +0x68, so the canvas is one more thing to put back and compare. A paint that makes the map's pixels cannot be repeated and is counted apart. Eight of the twelve functions ran: three of the five row routines (0x007A6A70, 0x007A6BD0, 0x007A6EE0, for other picture scales) and the paint's pixel routine (0x007A6FB0) did not, and are written but unverified.

To reach the other scales in a test: `OPENSC4_TEST_MINI_MAP=<day>` (token `minimap`, [minimap_autotest.cpp](../src/core/minimap_autotest.cpp)) makes a mini map of its own for each of five sizes (sides 32 to 512; the tutorial cities are 128 cells wide, so the scales -2 to 2), starts it as the game does (its area, then its Init), brings it up to date twice and paints it twice on a canvas of its own. 2026-10-11 on Big City, 7 days, with `renderer.minimap.draw=compare`: the 10 updates of the test redid 640 tiles, all five row routines ran, and neither they nor the test's 5 repeatable paints differ from the game's (the first paint on a new canvas cannot be repeated); the token is the same from the game alone and with the replacement on. The paint's pixel routine (0x007A6FB0) is only used for a canvas of format 7, sixteen bits with four of alpha, which a window gets only in a 16-bit full-screen video mode: it ran once in such a run, with no difference found, but the comparison does not read a canvas of that kind, so its output is not compared. Windowed runs always get a 32-bit canvas (format 9).

## Small windows

*Translation removed 2026-10-22: the game's own code runs again; a hand-written rewrite is pending (see "Translations removed" above). What follows describes the game's code and how the removed translation was checked.*

A window's paint (window slot 88) does not go to the driver either: it draws into the window's canvas, a `cIGZBuffer` the window keeps at +0x68, which the window manager puts on screen later. So a paint can be checked like the map pictures, in the same run: ours, then the object and the pixels of its buffers put back, then the game's, and the two results compared byte for byte (`PaintCompared` in `terrain_draw.cpp`).

The interface code left fourteen such functions as drawing (docs/ui.md). All are translated instruction by instruction and are behind `renderer.windows.paints`:

- The loading screen's two draws (0x00777220, 0x007773B0; its picture object keeps buffers at +0xC, +0x10, +0x14 and +0x98) with the blend they use (0x00776C30), and the overlay picture's paint (0x00794100, which copies its picture at +0xE4 into the canvas). A plain test run reaches these.
- The paints of the progress bar (0x00797CC0), the info tip (0x00798710), the animation recorder (0x007943B0) and its frame (0x00794BF0), the pop-up menu (0x0079AA70) and its arrows (0x0079B0E0), the intro window (0x0079D490) and the picture file dialog (0x0079D6B0), and the graph control's box (0x0076B660, a line function that forwards its seven arguments to the game's line drawing). Nothing puts these windows on screen in an unattended run; `OPENSC4_TEST_WINDOW_PAINTS=<day>` with `OPENSC4_TEST_TUTORIAL_OFF=1` (`src/core/window_paint_autotest.cpp`) brings each one up and has it painted, on the screen's canvas and again on a canvas of its own, which is a picture in memory.

The advisor head view's Draw (0x004209D0) is the one that is not a window paint: it goes to the driver through the 3D render, outside a frame, so it is compared like a pass (`DrawCompared`: the head view, the render's context and camera, and what reaches the driver). It is behind `renderer.windows.paints` with the others.

A window that makes its pictures anew in a paint (the info tip builds its text and its bubble when it is stale, at +0x140 and +0x144; the arrows build a strip) cannot simply be put back: the old picture is gone. `PaintComparedRebuilt` leaves such pointers as the run made them, puts back the rest of the object and the pixels of the buffers that stayed, and compares the rebuilt pictures by their size and pixels, not their address.

Checked on the three tutorial cities, with a self-check; screenshots match the game's (20 of 20). What the comparison saw:

- The mini map's paint: 149 paints, all of which drew, no difference.
- The eight window paints the test brings up (progress bar, info tip, recorder and frame, pop-up menu and arrows, intro, picture file dialog): 32 paints a run, 96 in all, no difference; 22 of the 32 change the object or a picture (the rest are the paints on the screen's canvas, which is not a picture in memory). The intro is never started in the test, so its paint only counts down a field: only the object is compared.
- The loading screen's draw: 12 calls a run, no difference, but only one a run changes anything in the object or its buffers, so this is a thin check.
- The overlay picture's paint: thousands of calls a run, no difference, and none of them had anything to draw (the paint leaves early without a picture), so only its way out is verified.
- The advisor head's Draw: twice a run in the test (two moods, with a model), 15 driver draws, no difference on the three cities; self-check clean.
- The graph box is not compared in the same run: the picture it is given is of another buffer class (vtable 0x00AC1550), which the comparison does not read. Its check is the test's own: the graph's canvas has the same checksum with the game's code and with ours, in separate runs.

The translator learned tail calls for this (a `jmp` out of the function or through a pointer: the callee runs without our return address and its result is ours), one-operand `mul`, the sign branches and every set-on-condition.

## The city view window

*Translation removed 2026-10-22: the game's own code runs again; a hand-written rewrite is pending (see "Translations removed" above). What follows describes the game's code and how the removed translation was checked.*

The window the city is shown in (`cSC4View3DWin`) is the interface code's, but five of its functions draw and were left to the renderer (docs/ui.md):

- **Paint** (0x007F6200, window slot 88) runs once a frame. It reads its stopwatch for the time since the last paint, steps a scroll in progress (0x007CD330), stores the time in what slot 0x28 of the object at +0xE8 gives (at +0x1C) and calls that object's slot 0x54, then takes one pending item off the list at +0x140. That the object at +0xE8 is the 3D render and slot 0x54 its draw is inferred from the slot numbers, not traced.
- **The mayor rating meter** (0x007E8510) loads the meter's picture, copies its rows up to the rating's level into a new buffer, hands that to the meter's window and places and shows the arrows beside it.
- **The lighting time** (0x007E6E40) turns the clock's hours and minutes into a float, gives it to the lighting manager and asks for a full redraw (a tail call).
- **The cost indicator's amount** (0x007EAC70) and **SetScrolling** (0x007F64F0).

All five are translated instruction by instruction. `renderer.cityview.draw` has the first three, which a test run reaches; `renderer.cityview.draw_untested` has the last two, which no test reaches, so that code has never run.

The paint cannot be run twice (it is a frame, on real time), so screenshots are its check, as for the render's own pass runner. The meter and the lighting time put their results into other objects, and give the same result when run again, so they have a new kind of same-run comparison (`EffectCompared`): ours runs first and notes every object it calls into; the first 128 bytes of each object that starts with a vtable are copied, and for a picture buffer its size and a sum of its pixels; then the game's runs twice. Bytes that differ between the game's two runs (addresses of things made anew, counts) are left out; the rest must be the same after ours as after the game's.

Checked on the three tutorial cities: screenshots match the game's (20 of 20 each); with the city running for 30 days the meter is compared twice a run and the lighting time once, over 14 and 4 objects, with no difference, and the self-check is clean. That is few calls: the meter only changes with the rating, and the lighting only when the clock's time is handed over.

For this the translator learned `adc` after an `add`, and to carry the flags of a jump to its target when the code above cannot fall into it; it reports where flags of different kinds meet and are then read. No branch of the translations already in the tree changes under the new rule.

To reach the last two in a test: `OPENSC4_TEST_CITY_DRAW=<day>` (token `cvdraw`, [cityview_draw_autotest.cpp](../src/core/cityview_draw_autotest.cpp)) sets the cost indicator five times through the view's interface (slot 42), fades it and switches it off (slot 44), and starts and stops the scrolling in the same tick (slot 33, four calls). `renderer.cityview.draw_untested` has no comparison of its own, so the token is the check: the amount's picture and the indicator's fields after every call, and the scroll fields after each SetScrolling. 2026-10-11 on Big City, 7 days: the amount drawn 5 times and SetScrolling called 4 times, with the same token from the game alone (twice), with the replacement on and in its compare mode. The test changes the advisors' part of the fingerprint from its day on, in the game alone as well; which of its calls does that was not looked into.

## The region view

*Translation removed 2026-10-22: the game's own code runs again; a hand-written rewrite is pending (see "Translations removed" above). What follows describes the game's code and how the removed translation was checked.*

The region screen's code is the Region work's (docs/region.md), which left its four draw functions to the renderer. Unlike the windows above these do go to the driver, so they are compared like the terrain passes, in the same frame (`DrawCompared`).

- **The picture grid** (`SC4RegionViewGrid::Draw`, 0x007B28B0): called in the 3D view's draw pass, it copies the grid of cached map pictures to the screen.
- **The terrain overlay** (`SC4RegionOverlay::Draw`, 0x007AB630): a render state, four vertices on its stack and one draw through the render context it is given.
- **The overlay window's paint** (`SC4RegionOverlayWin::Paint`, 0x007A9D60): the marks that drift over the region. Every frame it sets the driver up and draws each mark as a textured quad, then steps the marks by the time since the last frame, makes a new one with a chance of 0.003, and frees those past the right bound.
- **The mask of a city's picture** (`SC4CityFiles::DrawPictureMask`, 0x005DCCF0).

`renderer.region.draw` has all four. The first three run while the region screen is up; the mask is drawn when a city is saved, eight times a save, so a save test (`scripts/run-save-test.sh`, in a disposable folder) is what reaches it.

The region screen is not drawn in an unattended run: the test puts it up and loads the city at once. `OPENSC4_TEST_REGION_HOLD=<ms>` (with `OPENSC4_TEST_REGION_SCREEN=1`) keeps it on screen for that long first.

The overlay window's paint does not repeat by itself, so its comparison takes three steps:

- The marks' nodes are put back with the window between the two runs, and a frame that would free a mark is not repeated (counted as unrepeatable).
- The window's stopwatch keeps the clock's reading of its last restart, and the step moves every mark by the real time since then. Both are put back for the second run but cannot be compared byte for byte, since the second run starts later. The step is checked by its shape instead: in each run every mark must have moved by its speed times one same time.
- A frame in which a mark is made ends with a new node at a different address in each run. Such a frame shows as a divergence in the list's links. They are counted on their own, and the self-check has them too.

Checked with the region screen held for 20 seconds, three runs and a self-check:

- The overlay window's paint: 50,897 frames compared. The drawing, the window and the marks are the same in all but 144 of them, and exactly 144 frames made a mark; the self-check has 54 of each in 18,029 frames. The step has the right shape in every frame. 280 frames freed a mark and were not repeated.
- The picture grid and the terrain overlay are drawn once a run (the region's 3D view only redraws when something changes): no difference in the three runs, which is a thin check.
- The mask of a city's picture: a save of each tutorial city draws it eight times, so 24 masks are compared (8,072 driver draws in the Making Money save): no difference, and the self-check is clean.

There is no screenshot harness for the region screen, so the picture on screen is not compared.

## Water effects, sky and clouds

**What draws after the models.** Besides the models, the 3D render calls objects that registered for one of four passes (`cSC43DRender::AddViewObject`, 0x007C5D90: pass 0 and 2 around the static pass, 3 and 5 around the moving-objects pass; each object's slot 0xC with the render context, in the order number's order). `diagnostics.trace.viewobjects` logs every registration; `OPENSC4_VIEWOBJECT_SKIP=<draw function>` leaves an object out, to see in a screenshot what it draws. In a loaded city (2026-10-10):

| Pass, order | Draw function | What |
|---|---|---|
| 0, 0 and 2, 1000 | 0x0075BFD0 | the terrain view: land, then water |
| 2, 0 and 1100; 5, 1000 and 1100 | 0x00739490 | four overlay managers |
| 5, 1000 | 0x0046D990 | not identified yet (5.6 KB) |
| 5, 1000 | 0x007514F0 | the terrain view again: coloured and textured cell quads from two hash tables (+0xB0, +0x9C) |
| 5, 1000 (three objects) | 0x00620500 | not identified yet: quads from two trees, drawn twice |
| 5, 1100 | 0x00761FF0 | the shore: the surf line along the water's edge |
| 5, 1900 | 0x00593120 | the effects manager: every particle effect, the clouds and their shadows among them (below) |

There is no sky in the city view: what shows behind the terrain is the background the 3D render restores (`ClearViewport`). The clouds are particle effects: an effect's description carries a render flag (the effects parser's `kRenderWaterEffects` 3 and `kRenderCloudsAndFog` 4, the numbers of the render properties of those names), and with the effects manager left out the clouds and their shadows are gone and nothing else in the paused test city changes.

**The shore** (class name ours, `STEShore`: vtables 0x00AB45FC and 0x00AB4624, code 0x0075FAE0 to 0x00762100; the 3D render holds the interface at the second vtable). Its draw (0x00761FF0) builds the strips on the first draw (0x00761B70, from the terrain's cells whose record has flag 0x10 and not flag 1 at +0x22) and sets them up for the zoom (0x00761CF0, again whenever the context's zoom differs from +0x40). Then, when the `RenderWaterEffects` property is set (the byte at +0x7C of the render properties), it applies one fixed render state (0x00B4D528, filled on first use) and draws each group (0x38 bytes each, +0x34 to +0x38) whose bounding box (+0x20, +0x2C) the camera view has in sight: for each strip in the group's list (+0xC), again by its own box (+0x34, +0x40), the texture its index (+0xC) picks from the object's table (+0xB4) and its 24-byte vertices as one strip (primitive 7, vertex format 10; 0x0075FE70).

`renderer.water.shore` replaces the draw and the strip draw (building the strips and the zoom change still the game's). Compared draw by draw in the same frame as the terrain's passes; frames that build or change zoom run the game's only, because they allocate. 2026-10-10, three test cities with the camera workout and water effects on: 0 divergences (63,600, 64,584 and 81,191 calls of the draw; the game against itself also 0); 20 of 20 screenshots with ours on against the same run without it.

**The effects manager** (class name ours, `SC4EffectsManager`; the 3D render holds the interface at the object's +4, vtable 0x00A9F250; offsets below are from the interface). Its draw (0x00593120) does nothing when the render property at +0x39C is set. Else, after the height map's own lines are drawn (0x00586720 on the map at +0xD94, when +0xD98 is set; "The height map's rest" below), it empties two vectors and walks three lists of effects (index vectors at +0xBF0, +0xC38 and +0xC5C into the tables at +0xBE4, +0xC2C and +0xC50). An effect that is running (its slot 8) and whose box (0x005B26F0) the camera view has in sight, or whose description has flag 0x10, goes into the vector of {depth, effect} at +0xDA0, the depth being its position (+0x194) along the axis the rotation looks down, signed, plus an offset from its description (+0xD8); the others go into the vector at +0xDAC. The particle budget (+0xC9C) is then steered by the particle count of the last frame (+0xC8C) against the target from the render properties. The vector is sorted by depth (0x005906C0, 0x0058F980 and 0x0058E170; see "The sort by depth" below), and with the render property at +0x1BC set each effect in it is drawn (slot 0x24 with the frame's step, +0x1B8, and the render context) and its particles (+0x18) counted, and each effect out of view gets the same call without a context, last to first. A debug property (+0x35C) draws the boxes. Then every running entry of a fourth list (+0xBC0, index vector +0xBCC) is drawn (its slot 0x24), and three more steps follow: 0x0058E870 when +0xD44 is above 0 and the property at +0x3FC is set, 0x0058EC40 when +0xD88 is above 0, and 0x005922A0 always. The first two never run in the three test cities (coverage runs with the camera workout, 2026-10-13), nor does the helper's call at the start (0x00586720); the two are ours, compared only when forced ("The flash after the draw" and "The camera shake after the draw" below). The third is the clean-up (see "The clean-up after the draw" below).

What it draws in the test cities (`diagnostics.trace.effects` counts by class; Big City with clouds and water effects on): the sorted effects are of two classes (vtables 0x00AA0C58 and 0x00AA0EA0) that share one draw (0x005B8540), and the fourth list's entries are of one (vtable 0x00A9EE78, draw 0x00582920, about 250 a frame).

`renderer.effects.draw_manager` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_draw.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, `renderer.effects.draw`, removed 2026-10-22). Read closer: the depth is the position along the axis times the sign, unrounded, plus the description's +0xD8, rounded once; the third list's growth of the vector in view is the game's own inline copy of 0x007C61A0 (twice the size, at least one, from 0x0090CF54, the old storage freed), the other two call 0x007C61A0 and the vector out of view 0x0051CA60; a rotation outside 0 to 3 leaves the axis as the game's stack held it (ours the first, with 1). The budget: with the last frame's count (made unsigned) above the most (the render's +0x18's word at +0xE4), the budget times one less the blend (+0x24's +0xBC) times the most over the count, plus the blend; else, with the budget below the target (+0x24's +0x94), the most times one less the blend plus the count times the blend, times the budget, over the count when that stays below the count times the target, else the target; the comparisons go by the unrounded values and the count as a float. The sort's comparer is a word whose low byte the game clears; the rest is the count as a float, as its stack holds it. The draw cannot be run twice in a frame: effects and decals move on when drawn. Compare mode gives the two vectors room (so that neither run moves them), runs ours with the calls that draw or step something only noted (which, on what, with what: the helper's call, each effect's draw, the boxes' draw, each decal's step, 0x0058E870, 0x0058EC40 and the clean-up), puts the manager back, and runs the game's with the same calls noted at their entry (the effect's draw and the decal's step through their own detours, the others through detours of compare mode; the clean-up is checked as ours' last note) and made. Compared: the notes, what ours wrote of the manager (+0x1B4, +0x1B8, the count +0xC8C against the counts the game's effects had before they drew, +0xC94 to +0xC9C and the two vectors' words), that ours wrote nothing else, and the two vectors' contents. An effect's draw may add, take away or stop decals of the fourth list; a frame whose list (and which of it runs, slot 8) is not the same when the game's run steps it is compared without the decals' notes and their count (`effects.draw_manager.fourth_changed`). 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on, the four switches of this piece compared in the same runs: 0 divergences in 4,855, 6,742 and 12,653 frames (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour), and 42, 7 and 6 more with the fourth list changed. The first run found the decals stopped by an effect's draw (6 of 942 frames, the notes one decal short); the snapshot of the list now holds what runs. Not reached in the test cities (a count of each branch with ours on): the property at +0x39C, the helper's call, descriptions with flag 0x10, the boxes' draw, 0x0058E870 and 0x0058EC40, the drawing property (+0x1BC) clear, a rotation outside 0 to 3 and the third list's inline growth (the two calls to grow are reached with ours on, not in compare mode, which gives the vectors room); nor any of the budget's three ways: the count stays below the most (5,000) and the budget at the target (1). A compare run on Big City with the count or the budget set before both runs (a test build only) reached the first two ways in 41 and 82 frames, 0 divergences; the division needs a blend above zero, and the cities' is zero. Mistakes put in on purpose: the depth's sign and the decal count were reported, the budget without the blend not (the blend is zero).

**Forcing the manager's draw** (2026-10-25). With `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_MANAGER=1` (read only by `renderer.effects.draw_manager` in compare mode) each compared draw is made in turn with one of seven changes, which both runs see and which are put back after: none; the render's property at +0x39C set (the draw does nothing); its +0x1BC cleared (no effect drawn); its +0x35C set (each effect in view's box drawn in green); the blend 0.5 with the last frame's count 6,000, above the most (the budget's first way); the blend 0.5 with the budget 0.5, below the target (its second way); and flag 0x10 on every description of the three lists (no frustum test). The budget the draw leaves is put back as well, so that the forcing does not change later frames; the game's run draws on screen with the changes. A box's draw is now noted by its colour only: the box is the effect's, which the game's run has drawn (and so moved) before the boxes are drawn, and ours, whose effects' draws are only noted, has not. They are recorded as `effects.draw_manager.forced` and `effects.draw_manager.off.forced`. 2026-10-25, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on, every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours but the manager's draw: 0 divergences in 23,655 and 39,375 draws (Making Money, Rush Hour; 3,943 and 6,563 more with +0x39C set), and 1 in 14,199 on Big City, the particles counted 0x64 by the game's effects at their draw and 0x68 by ours: the same oddity a compare run of the manager's draw found once on 2026-10-23 (one effect's draw changing another's count within the frame, presumably). Mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 6 of 6 reported: the flag 0x10 test left out, the blend left out of the budget's first way, the count's share left out of its second, the boxes' colour with red, the +0x1BC test left out, the +0x39C test left out. Still not reached: a rotation outside 0 to 3 (the game's axis is then what its stack held), the third list's inline growth, and the flash and the shake (0x0058E870, 0x0058EC40), which have their own forcing.

**An effect** (class name ours, `SC4Effect`; two classes, vtables 0x00AA0EA0 and 0x00AA0C58, which differ in seven slots, among them the drawing without a texture, slot 0x44, and what is done when nothing is drawn, slot 0x4C). Its draw (0x005B8540, slot 0x24, shared by both; the step and a render context, null for an effect out of view) does nothing unless the effect runs (+9). It adds the step to the effect's clock (+0x110; when +0x118 is set, once, plus the description's +0x1C), asks the object at +0x1F0 for a value (+0x1FC), and for descriptions with flags runs extra steps (0x005B7030 for 0x2000, the start edge; 0x005B8410 for 0x80000000, the starts at set ages; both below). The velocity of the frame (+0x13C) is the description's (+0xE0) plus the effect's own (+0x150) plus one along a path when the description has one (0x005B6C40, else zero) plus a vector the effect points to (+0x1E8, at its +8) times a factor of the description (+0xEC). Then every particle of the list at +0x14 (nodes of next, previous and the particle) whose first byte says it runs is stepped (the class's slot 0x50, 0x005B9BA0: its age against its life, its position by the velocity); one that has ended is unlinked, its node freed, the count (+0x18) lowered and the particle given back through the sub-object at +4 (slot 0x40). New particles are emitted (slot 0x3C, 0x005B96E0, unless +0x21A). With a render context and particles left it draws: slot 0x38, a context call for descriptions with flag 1 (0x007D31B0, undone after the drawing by 0x007D3290), the render state at +0x1C, then slot 0x48 (0x005B4B90) when +0x1EC is set, else slot 0x44; without, slot 0x4C. Last the sub-object is asked whether the effect is over (0x005B9D40), and if not, descriptions with flag 0x800000 get one more step (0x005B2730).

`renderer.effects.draw_effect` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_draw.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, `renderer.effects.effect`, removed 2026-10-22). Read closer: the step (and the description's +0x1C, once) is added at double precision, kept rounded at +0x108 and added unrounded to the clock; the velocity's sums are rounded to float before the vector at +0x1E8 times the factor is added, but for the third axis, whose first sum is rounded before the pull is added and whose product stays unrounded; the particle loop's end is read anew each time. Compared by record and replay as the emitters (`RecordAndReplay`): the game's run with its calls recorded and the particle's step answered, ours replayed. Recorded: 0x005B7030, the pull, 0x005B8410, the step, the give back, the emit, the axes, the context's four calls (0x007D31B0, 0x007D2D20, 0x007D4530, 0x007D3290), the three particle draws, the two classes' slot 0x4C, the end check and the wandering. The seven with switches of their own (the step, the emit, the axes, the box, the wandering, the pull and the second class's step out of view) are recorded by their own detours while an effect's draw is compared (`g_drawRecording`); the others get detours in compare mode, which record only then. The value the object at +0x1F0 gives (slot 0x74) is asked again in the replay; the node's free is held back as the other comparisons' are. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (the four of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 185,696, 312,498 and 811,680 draws (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour). Not reached: descriptions with flags 0x2000, 0x80000000 and 1 (the colour) and an effect with +0x21A set (the two extra steps have their own forced comparison since, below). Mistakes put in on purpose: the count not lowered and the two draws swapped were reported; the third axis's first sum not rounded and the pull not cleared were not (the values give the same floats; the pull is zero already).

**A particle's step** (0x005B9BA0, slot 0x50 of both effect classes: the particle and the step).
