A particle starts with a byte that says it runs, then its age (+4) and life (+8), its position (+0xC) and its velocity (+0x1C). The step adds the step's time to the age and, when the age has reached the life, clears the first byte and answers false. Else the velocity takes the effect's velocity of the frame (+0x13C) times the step and is multiplied by a constant (0x00A81228) minus the step times a value of the description (+0x100), and the position moves by the step times a constant (0x00A92D28) times the sum of the old and the new velocity. Descriptions with a flag of the mask at 0x00B22E14 are stepped by another function (0x005B9790) instead.

`renderer.effects.step` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_life.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). The game keeps some of the step's sums on the x87 stack and stores others first: the first component's new velocity is used as stored, the other two as worked out, and the first component's sum of old and new is stored before it is scaled. It writes only the particle, so compare mode runs ours and the game's on the same particle (`SuCompared`); a step that goes on to the other step is left to that one's comparison. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all seven of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 1,992,500, 5,111,042 and 6,008,078 steps. A mistake put in on purpose in the move was reported.

**An effect's emit** (0x005B96E0, slot 0x3C of both effect classes) does nothing when +0x10 is set. A countdown at +0x190 is lowered by the frame's step (+0x108), and while it stays above zero nothing is emitted; once it is at or below zero, the time to emit for is what it went below zero by (or the whole step when it was there already), held to a limit from the description (+0x1C) when that is set. That time goes to one of three emitters by the description's flags: 0x005B3CF0 for flag 4, 0x005B8B50 for flag 2, else 0x005B87B0.

`renderer.effects.emit` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_life.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22); the three emitters have their own switches (below). Everything the emit does itself comes before its one call, so compare mode runs ours with that call only noted (which emitter, the time and the effect as it was then), puts the effect back and runs the game's, with detours on the three emitters (in compare mode only) noting the same at their entry; when neither emits, the effects are compared. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all seven of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 1,469,805, 1,691,891 and 2,403,993 emits. A mistake put in on purpose in the time handed on was reported.

**The emitter that keeps a count** (0x005B3CF0, for descriptions with flag 4). The time it is given, times a rate (a double at +0x130), is added to a double at +0x120; nothing more happens until that has passed the integer at +0x128. Then a count is worked out from the first float of the description's +0x88 (rounded by adding two constants and reading the float's bits, one less when that overshot), and the number of particles (+0x18) is brought up to it: each missing one is taken from the effect's sub-object (its slot 0x3C, the particle pool at 0x00B0A688 behind it) and, when its first byte is set, added to the list (slot 0x2C), else given back (slot 0x40).

`renderer.effects.emit_count` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_emit.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). The count rounded up is the description's first value plus the float at 0x00A84D2C plus 1.5 times 2^23, rounded to a float, its bits less 0x4B400000, one less when the value was exactly that less one; the particles missing are counted down as the game's unsigned loop does. It is compared by record and replay like the effect's draw, whose machinery serves any function of an effect: the pool's functions are recorded in the game's run and answered from the record in ours, and because a particle that was given back no longer has the first byte the game's run read (the pool keeps a link there), the byte of the record is put into it for the replay and taken out again after. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, the three emitters and the emit compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 61,531, 65,380 and 278,266 calls. A mistake put in on purpose in the number taken was reported.

**The emitter that emits a steady stream** (0x005B87B0, for descriptions with neither flag 4 nor flag 2). For each particle that is due within the time it is given, one is taken from the sub-object (slot 0x3C). When the particle's first byte is set it is stepped at once by the time since it was due (the class's slot 0x50) and, if it is still running after that, added to the list (slot 0x2C), after 0x005B84C0 for descriptions with flag 0x80000000 (which goes through the description's table at +0x1CC for further effects to start at the particle); a particle that is not running or has ended already is given back (slot 0x40). What is left of the time, for some descriptions less a random delay (the generator at 0x00B22DF8, between two values of the description at +0x28), goes negated into the countdown at +0x190 that the emit counts down; one path ends the emission through the effect's slot 4.

`renderer.effects.emit_stream` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_emit.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). It works in slices of at most the float at 0x00AA0CF4: each adds the slice times the rate to +0x120, and passing the count plus one moves the count on; at the description's most (+0x18) an effect without flag 8 is stopped (slot 4 with 0), else, with the description's +0x28 not below the float at 0x00A81054, the slice is cut short and from then on each slice ends with a pause drawn between +0x28 and +0x2C. The number of particles is the description's list at +0x88 read at the part past the count (between two neighbours), times the slice and a scale (the target of +0x1E8 times +0x1E0 and +0x1C4, times +0xC0 once, twice or three times for flags 0x80000, 0x100000, 0x200000), added to +0x138, which keeps what is below a whole one. Compared by record and replay as the count emitter, with two additions while an emitter is compared: the particle's step (0x005B9BA0, slot 0x50) is recorded and its answer replayed, and the emitter's own draws from the generator are noted in the game's run (a detour on 0x00913761, compare mode only, that notes only the draws made by the emitter itself on its thread) and handed to ours in the replay, as calls whose two bounds are compared. The emit's comparison and this one share one detour per emitter. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, the three emitters and the emit compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 1,045,179, 1,036,480 and 1,509,252 calls. A mistake put in on purpose in what +0x138 keeps was reported.

**The emitter that emits in bursts** (0x005B8B50, to 0x005B8DBA, for descriptions with flag 2 and not 4) keeps the clock of the count emitter (the time times the rate at +0x130 added to the double at +0x120, against +0x128) and calls what the stream emitter calls: a particle taken, stepped (slot 0x50), added after 0x005B84C0 or given back, a random number drawn from the generator at 0x00B22DF8, and the effect's slot 4 on one path.

`renderer.effects.emit_burst` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_emit.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). A burst's size is the description's first value at +0x88 (times the scale of the stream without its powers, when above the float at 0x00AA0F1C and the description's +8 has no bit 0), rounded up as the count emitter's; each round takes that many particles when the part past the count times +0x14 is below +0x10, all stepped by that same time, and a pause drawn between +0x28 and +0x2C times the rate is taken off +0x120. Compared as the stream emitter. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, the three emitters and the emit compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 15,110 and 24,683 calls (Rush Hour has no effect that uses it). A mistake put in on purpose in the time the particles are stepped by was reported. With this the three emitters are ours.

**Before an effect draws** (0x005B3B90, slot 0x38 of both effect classes, with the render context): when the view's rotation (the context's +0x18) is not the one the effect keeps at +0x88, the two axes its particles are drawn along (+0x70 and +0x7C, three floats each) are taken again. They are the camera view's two (the object at the context's +0x24, its +0x60 and +0x6C), or, for descriptions whose byte at +0xD5 is 1, two worked out from the description's three floats at +0x30 (scaled by the value at 0x00B0A664 over a length; fixed axes when that length is below the limit at 0x00AA0D00).

`renderer.effects.axes` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_setup.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). It writes only the effect, so compare mode runs ours and the game's on the same effect. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all nine of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 11,736, 271,275 and 418,504 calls (most find the rotation unchanged). A mistake put in on purpose in the axes worked out from the description was reported in all three cities.

**Drawing the particles.** A count over the three test cities with everything on (`OPENSC4_EFFECT_CENSUS=1` counts the calls of every hooked effect function) shows which of the three particle draws they use: the one into the driver's dynamic vertex buffer (0x005B4B90, slot 0x48, about 50,000 calls a run) and the second class's own (0x005ADD50, slot 0x44, 2,000 to 6,000). The base class's draw without that buffer (0x005B3DA0) is never called, nor are the extra steps for descriptions with flag 0x2000 (0x005B7030) or 0x80000000 (0x005B8410), which are compared by forcing them (below, "The start edge and the starts at set ages").

The draw into the dynamic buffer (0x005B4B90 to 0x005B637F; used when the effect has one, +0x1EC) does nothing without a texture (+0x68). It binds the texture, starts the box around the particles (+0x1A0 to +0x1B4), and draws the particles in runs of at most the buffer's capacity (its slot 5, a quarter of it in quads): the effect's batch (+0x28) is emptied and given the run's indices, the buffer is locked for the run's vertices (slot 6), each particle of the list becomes four 0x20-byte vertices (worked out from the particle, the description's curves and the two axes at +0x70 and +0x7C; the arithmetic is written out with ours below), then the buffer is unlocked (slot 8) and drawn with the batch's indices (slot 10). The game guards the filling with an exception handler that unlocks and resets the buffer after a fault. Last the box is widened by a value from the description (+0xAC).

`renderer.effects.draw_buffered` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_particles_draw.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, `renderer.effects.particles_buffered`, removed 2026-10-22), without the exception handler. Read closer: a particle's `t` is its age over its life rounded to a float, and each of the description's lists is read at `t` with the place (the last index times `t`) rounded to a float for the test of the rounding down and for the part, the part tested unrounded and multiplied rounded, the value kept as a float. The colour is the list of three floats at +0x94 read so (each value's two parts rounded before they are added; the rest, one less the part, rounded to a float for the red and green, not for the blue) times the particle's +0x38 to +0x40; the opacity its +0x34 times +0x1D0 times the list at +0xA0; a spread the list at +0xB8 times its +0x2C; the size the list at +0xAC times its +0x28, at most the vector at +0x1E8's +0x34; the turn the list at +0xC4 times its +0x30 plus the description's +0x74. The place (+0xC) is turned about the y axis around the effect's +0xB4 by its height above +0xB8 times the description's +0x104 when that is not zero (the sine and cosine rounded to floats, the turned offsets added back to the centre), then moved by 0x005AC070 for a description with a list at +0x108 (with its +0x14 times the effect's double at +0x120). The two axes (+0x7C, +0x70) are, for a description whose +0xDC is not zero, turned to face along the particle's velocity (+0x1C): its parts along each, their length (the second's part unrounded times rounded, plus the first's squared), each part over the length (the first with the unrounded inverse, the second with it rounded to a float) mixing the axes into their sum and difference, and the first stretched by the length over +0xDC when that is above it. The first axis is scaled by the size, the second by the size times the spread (unrounded for its x, rounded for the others), both turned by the turn times 2 pi (the float at 0x00AA0D04, the sine and cosine rounded), and for an effect whose second block has its second byte set (+0xC5) the place is taken through that block (its rows at +0xC8, scaled by +0xF8, plus its place +0xEC) and both axes scaled by +0xF8. The corner is the place less half the axes' sum (the float at 0x00A84D2C); the four vertices are the corner plus the first axis, the corner, the corner plus the second and that plus the first, each with the colour (bytes of blue, green, red and opacity, each the float at 0x00AA0CFC added and its bits taken off, less itself over 256) and the texture places (0, 0), (0, 1), (1, 1) and (1, 0), the second values turned over for flag 0x40000000 of the description's first word and the first values of the first and third corners for flag 0x20000000; the last eight bytes of each vertex are left as they were. The box is widened by the place. The list is followed for the count (+0x18) without looking for its end. Every other product and sum is rounded to a float. It cannot run twice (it draws), so it is compared by the notes' engine, as the second class's step out of view is: the buffer gets a copy of its table of functions whose lock, unlock and draw note their calls, and a detour of compare mode notes the texture bind; the game's runs with the calls passed on, the vertices it wrote read from the locked memory at the unlock (the first 0x18 bytes of each); the effect is put back (but for its batch, +0x28 to +0x68, which both runs empty and fill alike with the game's functions, and whose storage the game's run may have moved) and ours runs with each call answered from the notes, writing into memory the replay lends it. Compared: the calls, the effect and the vertices. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on, the three particle draws compared and the eight switches of the second class (`pool_b`, `giveback_b`, `notdrawn_b`, `block_b`) and of the draws without particles ours in the same runs: 0 divergences in 283,471, 66,255 and 181,529 draws (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour). Not reached in the test cities (a count of each branch with a test build): an effect without a texture, the two texture flags, a failed lock, more than one run, a colour read exactly on a value of a longer list, the turn about the y axis, 0x005AC070, the axes along the velocity and their stretch, and the size at its most. Mistakes put in on purpose (a test build): the texture places of the last two corners swapped, the colour's red and blue swapped, the corner without the half and the opacity without its list were reported.

`renderer.effects.draw_plain` replaces the base class's other draw (0x005B3DA0 to 0x005B4B82, slot 0x44), written by hand in `src/core/effects_particles_draw.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, `renderer.effects.particles`, removed 2026-10-22). It does what the buffered draw does, into the effect's batch: nothing without a texture; the box started empty; the batch emptied (0x005EC4A0) and given the vertices (0x005EC8C0) and the indices (0x005EC850) of a quad for each of the count; each particle of the list (to its end) worked out as there, but for the arithmetic, which this optimised copy keeps more of on the x87: `t` and the places where the lists are read stay unrounded (the colour read as the second class's draw reads it, the part rounded to a float), the place is turned with the cosine rounded and the sine not, the first axis's x stays unrounded through the velocity, the size, the spread, the turn and the second block, the second axis's x through the velocity and the turn, and the spread's product, the second block's rows but the z and the halves but the x are not rounded. The box is widened by the place. A quad whose opacity byte is below the double at 0x00A92688 (0.01) is not written; the others are written one after another as four 0x18-byte vertices of place and colour (the texture places left as they were). Last, with vertices in the batch (+0x24), the texture is bound (0x007D4070) and the batch drawn (0x005EC5D0), and the box grown as the buffered draw's. Compared as the buffered draw is, the batch's vertices read at its draw by a detour of compare mode. It is never called in the test cities as they run.

**Forcing the two base class draws** (2026-10-25). With `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_DRAWS=1` (read only by `renderer.effects.draw_buffered` and `renderer.effects.draw_plain` in compare mode) each compared draw is made in turn with one of eight changes, which both runs see and which are put back after: none; the description's turn about the y axis (+0x104) 0.002; its axes along the velocity (+0xDC) 0.5, so that fast particles are stretched, or 1,000, so that none is; both texture flips (flags 0x40000000 and 0x20000000; the plain draw has none); the most size (the vector at +0x1E8's +0x34) 0.5, so that sizes are clamped; flip 0x20000000 alone; and no texture (+0x68), so that nothing is drawn. The game's run draws them on screen too. They are recorded as `effects.draw_buffered.forced` and `effects.draw_plain.forced`. 2026-10-25, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on, the two compared with the second class's four and the draws without particles ours: 0 divergences in 58,655, 104,278 and 117,722 buffered draws and, with `UseVertexBuffers=0`, 205,733, 157,944 and 222,007 plain draws (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour). Mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 8 of 8 reported: the buffered draw's flip bits swapped, its turn's sine subtracted the other way, its stretch left out, its size clamp left out, its velocity axes' second axis turned the other way; the plain draw's turn the other way, its velocity axes' second axis the other way and its size clamp left out. Still not reached: a failed lock, more than one run, a colour read exactly on a value of a longer list, 0x005AC070 (a list at +0x108, whose layout is not known), and the second class draw's own branches (`renderer.effects.draw_b`), which this forcing does not touch.

**Forcing the second class's draw** (2026-10-26). The same variable now also reaches `renderer.effects.draw_b` in compare mode: each compared draw is made in turn with one of six changes, put back after both runs: none; the description's flag 1 (bit 4 of the instance's colour); its turn about y (+0x104) 0.002; flag 2 of its third word (each particle asked by 0x005AD890 whether it is in view); a made-up list of three colours at +0x94, so that each colour is read between two; and flags 8 and 0x10 of its third word (the particle's own effect given the opacity, slot 0x30, and a placed block, slot 0x1C). Recorded as `effects.draw_b.forced`. Three test cities with the camera workout, all other effects switches ours: 0 divergences in 30,381, 40,107 and 58,420 draws (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour). Mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 6 of 6 reported: flag 1's colour bit, the turn's sine the other way, a particle out of view drawn, the next colour's green from its blue, the opacity given as value 2, and the placed block's place from the wrong row. Not forced: a model of more than one frame (no test city has one, and flags 0x20 and 0x40 and +0x1DC count only there), and the part's start and step (flags 4 and 0x100): forcing them on an effect whose part was not made for them crashes the game's own step (0x005AC200, reading through a null pointer at +0x114). The part's start is reached without forcing in Big City.

**The third effect class: its life cycle** (2026-10-26, part E10a). The manager makes an effect of a third class (tables of functions 0x00AA0B88 and 0x00AA0B40, 0x280 bytes) for a description with flag 1 whose float at +0x1D8 is not zero; no test city has one, so none of its code runs there. Its own code is about 45 functions and 20 KB (0x005A6200 to 0x005AB2F0). From what they do it is an effect built on a model rather than a texture: its description's +0xD0 names a model (type 0x5AD0E817, group 0xBADB57F1), which is held at +0x254 with the textures of its materials in a vector at +0x248 and a 0x40-byte batch for each in a vector at +0x230; two large functions (0x005A9150, 0x005A9820, with a recursive one at 0x005A9FF0) cut the model's triangles into 0x3C-byte pieces, and its particles' step (0x005A6520) pushes them out from a point (+0x25C) with a front (+0x26C) that grows at the description's +0x1EC and falls off exponentially: something blown apart. Its own draws are 0x005A6990 and 0x005A77F0. This part makes its life cycle ours: the maker (`SC4EffectsManager::NewEffectC`, 0x00590EA0, in `renderer.effects.makers`; it is NewEffectB's with its list at +0xC54, +0xC60, +0xC6C), the constructor (0x005A8ED0, in `renderer.effects.ctor`), the destructor and its deleting wrapper (0x005A90A0, 0x005A9080, in `renderer.effects.dtor`), and in a new switch `renderer.effects.third` the sub-object's letting go (0x005A6310: the model released, then the base class's), its giving back of a particle (0x005A6200, which pushes it onto a list at 0x00B0A5A0, not the pool at 0x00B0A688 the base class's take takes from) and the class's setting of a value by id (0x005AAC90: 8 the point at +0x25C, 9 handed to 0x005AA700, 0x101 the word at +0x278, any other the base class's 0x005B2490). The destructor's comparison is the other two's with three more things seen: each batch's destruction through a copy of its table of functions, the destructor's own frees (noted through the shared detour of SC4::Free and held back until ours has read the vectors) and the textures of +0x248 (each given one more holder for the game's run, which must take exactly one). With `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_THIRD=1` (compare mode of `renderer.effects.third`) one of six turns runs before each manager's draw: one made by the manager's maker and its index moved straight back to the free ones; that one's values set with made-up words (ids 8, 0x101, 0, 0x102, 0x20, put back after); that one let go in use (with a made-up model, the objects at +0x1F0 and +0x1EC the base class releases held once more first) and not in use; a made-up particle and a null one given back; a new one constructed and deleted empty; and one deleted with its vectors filled (a block, a vector of words, two batches copied from its own, up to two textures held by effects of the first class, the made-up model). Three test cities with the camera workout and those four switches compared (parity 12/12 with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours): 0 divergences in 13,809/13,809/6,905/6,904/34,525 (Big City), 2,251/2,251/1,127/1,126/5,630 (Making Money) and 5,561/5,561/2,781/2,780/13,905 (Rush Hour) constructions, destructions, lettings go, givings back and values set, with the makers' comparison seeing the forced makings too. Seven mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 7 of 7 reported: the constructor's 10.0 as 9.0, the destructor forgetting one free, freeing the batches' storage before destroying them, the letting go leaving the field, the giving back onto the base class's pool, id 8's first two words swapped and id 0x101 answering false. Not reached even forced: id 9 (its 0x005AA700 would run for real in the game's run) and the making of a new one with room in the list (the first making grows it, and that is not compared). The rest of the class (its description, model, pieces, step, emit and draws) is still the game's; the plan is in `effects-automata-drawing.md`.

**The third effect class: its description and model** (2026-10-26, part E10b). A new switch `renderer.effects.model_c` (written by hand in `src/core/effects_third.h`) has the class's two entries for them, slot 0x28 (an effect given its description, 0x005AB190) and slot 0x2C (an effect given a model's instance, 0x005AAFE0), and everything only they reach: the model's load by its ID (0x005AB0F0: the resource of type 0x5AD0E817, group 0xBADB57F1, whose slot 0x18 gives the model's materials at +0x254), the materials kept with the texture of each drawn material held in the vector at +0x248 (0x005AAE80), and the words and batches made from those textures (0x005AAD70: a word for each texture, the first word its first word points at; one 0x40-byte batch each, set up by 0x005EC7C0 with 4/6 and 0x005EC430 while +0x22C is set, else 3/0), with the game's std::vector code for the textures and the batches written out (0x005AAD00, 0x005AAC40, 0x005AA290, 0x005A8C30 and the helpers 0x005A6420, 0x005A6450, 0x005A8FF0, 0x005A9D00, 0x005A9D80, 0x005A63B0, 0x005A8D40, 0x005A88C0, 0x005A8E90). So +0x254 is the materials object, not the model itself. The description holds the description, sets the render state by its kind, the clock's rate (one over +0x14 or a floor of 1e-6), clears +0x22C, +0x258 and the box +0x194..+0x1B4, makes +0x268 the description's +0xF0 squared times 1024 and, for a model's ID at +0xD0 (not 0 or -1), loads it and takes the point +0x25C from +0xB4; the instance's entry keeps its materials and makes +0x25C the middle of its box (slot 0x20) with y a tenth of the way up from the bottom. No test city makes such an effect, so compare mode runs the game's only, and with `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_THIRD=1` one of two turns runs before each manager's draw on a pair of new effects of the class (the game's function on one, ours on the other, compared after each step by their bytes, the vectors' contents and room, each batch's words and vectors, and what each run changed of the descriptions', textures' and materials' counts): real descriptions with their fields changed for the step (without flag 1; with a model's ID taken from a second-class description, loaded for real; the same again; another with ID 0 and a zero rate; ID -1 and a NaN rate; an ID no resource has), and made-up instances with made-up materials and textures (three with one not drawn and one without a texture, five, two, five, five, nine, none), so that the vectors grow, shrink and are refilled with room. Textures a real model's materials hold are compared from the turn after they are first seen (they are held from then on so that they stay). The destructor's comparison (`renderer.effects.dtor`) now holds the model's materials once more for the game's run, as a real model's materials let go of their own textures when released for the last time. Three test cities with the camera workout and the third class's five switches compared: 0 divergences in 8,325/11,655 (Big City), 29,700/41,580 (Making Money) and 54,185/75,852 (Rush Hour) descriptions and instances given, with the constructor, destructor and textures' comparisons at 0 alongside. Ten mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 10 of 10 reported: +0x268 times 512, +0x22C not cleared, the rate's test the other way round, the loaded point's z from +0xB8, an undrawn material's texture kept, new materials not held, the batch set up with 3 for +0x22C, the texture's word not followed, y at the middle, and the batches growing only by the new ones. Parity 12/12 with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours. Not reached even forced: an insert or erase of batches anywhere but the end (only 0x005AAD00 calls them, at the end).

**The third effect class: its pieces** (2026-10-27, part E10c). A new switch `renderer.effects.pieces_c` (written by hand in `src/core/effects_third.h`) has the code that blows the class's model apart, which only the class's emit (0x005AA5D0, through 0x005A9E10 and 0x005AA3A0, still the game's) calls: a quad cut into a grid of count by count pieces (`SC4EffectC::CutGrid`, 0x005A9150), a triangle halved across its longest side (the later one for a tie) at the side's middle, the new point and texture place put at the end of their vectors, until that side is shorter than a limit (`SC4EffectC::SplitTriangle`, 0x005A9FF0, recursive), one piece of a triangle (`SC4EffectC::CutTriangle`, 0x005A9820) and the particle made for each piece (`SC4EffectC::MakeParticle`, 0x005A8930), with the pieces' vector code written out (0x005A8D90, 0x005A63F0, 0x005A6480, 0x005A6220, 0x005A6340). A piece is 0x3C bytes at +0x220: the way from its middle back to its first corner, its two sides and three texture places, all taken through the effect's matrix (+0x90), place (+0xB4) and scale (+0xC0). Its particle comes from the list at 0x00B0A5A0 (the pool 0x00B0A594 of 0x48-byte particles grown when empty), placed at the piece's middle, its byte +0x18 the material's number and +0x44 the piece's, with a direction, speed, push away from the description's point +0xF4 (by +0xF0, none within 1e-4 of it), life and three scales drawn from the generator at 0x00B22DF8 by the description's ranges; +0x34 is never written. With `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_THIRD=1` in compare mode one turn of three before each manager's draw runs the game's and ours on a pair of new effects of the class with the same made-up matrix, description (four kinds by turn) and generator state, the sub-object's slot 0x2C replaced by one that notes the particles: particles made straight away (one on the description's point, one within a hair of it), grids of 2, 1, 0 and 3, four triangles' pieces, four triangles halved (one with a tie, the vectors full or with room) and the vector code on its own (putting pieces in the middle, assigning down, copying); in one turn of eight (eight times) the free list is emptied for both runs so that the pool grows. Compared by the effect's bytes, the pieces, the particles' bytes, the halving's vectors and the generator after: 0 divergences in 37,756/388/21,368 grids (Big City/Making Money/Rush Hour) with as many particle steps and 37,756/388/21,368 halvings and triangles, the class's other five switches compared alongside at 0. Thirteen mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 10 reported: missed were the turn's products added in another order, the push's z square from the rounded z and the hair's test on the unrounded sum, all of which only change a float's last bit in rare cases. Parity 12/12 with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours. Left of the class: its step 0x005A6520, emit 0x005AA5D0 with 0x005AA3A0, 0x005A9E10 and value 9's 0x005AA700 (E10d), and its two draws (E10e).

**The third effect class: its emit and step** (2026-10-31, part E10d). A new switch `renderer.effects.emit_c` (written by hand in `src/core/effects_third.h`) has the rest of the class's particle code: its emit (`SC4EffectC::Emit`, 0x005AA5D0, slot 0x3C), the two ways it blows the model apart the first time (`SC4EffectC::CutTriangles`, 0x005AA3A0: each triangle of each primitive of each drawn material's mesh halved by SplitTriangle until its sides are below 16 over the size; `SC4EffectC::CutQuads`, 0x005A9E10, when +0x22C is set: every four of the mesh's vertices cut into a grid as many a side as the size truncated), its particles' step (`SC4EffectC::StepParticle`, 0x005A6520, slot 0x50) and value 9's handler (`SC4EffectC::SetPointAlong`, 0x005AA700). The size is the root of the float +0x1E8 points at times +0x1C4, times the description's +0x1D8. Both cuts gather the mesh's vertices (places and texture places, `GDVertexFormat::Gather`) once for each of its primitives, though only the triangles' walker (0x0080AE60, 0x0080B320, 0x0080B420, 0x0080AE70, called at their addresses) looks at the primitive; the quads cut all the vertices again each time. The emit: without materials or with +0x188 set, the effect is stopped once it says it is done; else a wait (+0x190) is counted down by the step (+0x108), the first time (+0x258 clear, the clock +0x120 at zero) the front +0x26C and its strength +0x274 are cleared and the model cut, last frame's front is kept at +0x270, and the clock moves by its rate and is held below one (0x00AA0BE0). The step: a front speed of zero (the description's +0x1EC) hands over to the base class's; else once a frame the front grows and its strength is read off the description's curve (+0x1F4) at the clock's fraction; a particle not yet aged and not yet reached by the front is left as it is; else it ages (ending at its life), is pulled (+0xE0), blown (+0xEC times the effect's +0x1B8), pushed away from the point +0x25C by the strength over its distance times the difference of two fall-offs, (1/k − 1 + d)·e^(−kd)/k at its distance behind this frame's front and last frame's (over +0x1F0, at least zero; k the description's +0x200), slowed (+0x100) and moved by half the step times the old velocity and the new. The game takes e^x on the x87 (FLDL2E, FRNDINT, F2XM1, FSCALE), whose last bits are the processor's, so ours runs the same instructions (`EcExp`). Value 9 gathers every material's mesh's vertices (drawn or not, once a primitive) into one vector (`SC4TripleVector::InsertN`), moves them by the effect's place (+0xB4 through the matrix at +0xC8, times +0xF8, plus +0xEC), takes each along the direction and puts +0x25C on the line at the middle of the least and the most, plus +0x278. With `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_THIRD=1` in compare mode one turn of three before each manager's draw runs the game's and ours on a pair of new effects of the class set up as for the pieces, with the same materials: a real model's (08100012 in Big City and Making Money, AAA402B4 in Rush Hour), three made-up ones made of its first mesh (drawn, not drawn, its primitives twice) or two with its vertices cut to a multiple of four. The emit the first time by triangles and by quads (26 to 2,778 pieces), again with the front grown, waiting, done waiting, at the clock's end, stopped and without materials; the step on ten made-up particles (the front grown at a fraction of the clock, a whole clock and near its end, not grown, a particle not reached, one reached while new, one ending, one on the point, one within a hair of it, one far behind), with a curve of one value or four and in one turn of four a front speed of zero; value 9's point along two directions, with and without +0x278 and without materials, then value 9 asked through the class's table. Compared by the effect's bytes, its pieces, the particles made, each made-up particle's bytes and the step's answer, and the generator after: 0 divergences in 19,252/6,948/21,052 emits (Big City/Making Money/Rush Hour; with 4,813/1,737/5,263 waits and 9,626/3,474/10,734 stopped), 48,130/17,370/54,710 steps and 19,252/6,948/21,052 points, the class's other switches compared alongside at 0 (the `set_value_c.9` comparison, which E10a could not reach, among them). Eighteen mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 17 reported: missed was e^x taken by the C library instead of the x87's sequence, which gave the same bits on these inputs. Parity 12/12 with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours (the final build, whose only change is in the forcing, gave Big City d1095's first run of the B side a different day even with B running everything as the game's, so not from this code); the three cities' shots match main's. Not reached even forced: value 9's vector cut down (it only grows) and a mesh with no primitives. Left of the class: its two draws, 0x005A6990 (slot 0x44) and 0x005A77F0 (slot 0x48) (E10e).

**The third effect class: its draws** (2026-10-31, part E10e). A new switch `renderer.effects.draw_c` (written by hand in `src/core/effects_third.h`) has the class's two draws. `SC4EffectC::Draw` (0x005A6990, slot 0x44, with the render context): before the emit has blown the model apart (+0x258 clear), with materials, the model is drawn whole: the context's model transform (0x007D3400) is the second block (+0xC8, rows, place and scale, with its byte +0xC5), the first (+0x90) when only the first's byte +0x8D is set, or with both set the second taken after the first (the first's place through the second's rows times its scale plus its place, the rows multiplied, the scales multiplied, the bytes or'd); then each material is set up (0x007D5310) with properties of two clear bytes, the floats at 0x00B0A580 and one, and its mesh drawn (0x007D4A80), and +0x18 cleared. Else the batches (+0x230, one for each drawn material's texture) are emptied and each particle of the list writes its piece (+0x220, by its +0x44) into its batch (by its byte +0x18): three 0x18-byte vertices (place, colour, texture place), a fourth for a batch of quads (+8 four). With `t` its age over its life, the description's lists read at `t` give a turn (+0xC4 times the particle's +0x30, times 0x00AA0B2C), a size (+0xAC times 0x00A9A414), the colour (+0x94, times the effect's +0x1D4 to +0x1DC) and the opacity (+0xA0); the corner is the particle's place plus the piece's first three floats, its two sides the piece's next six times the size, turned by the turn. Each batch with vertices then has its texture's word (+0x23C) bound and is drawn (0x005EC5D0). `SC4EffectC::DrawBuffered` (0x005A77F0, slot 0x48; its context is not used) writes the same pieces as 0x20-byte vertices into the dynamic vertex buffer (+0x1EC) in runs of its room (slot 0x14 over four), through the first batch only and binding no texture; its arithmetic is kept to floats, its colour has no scales and the place is first turned about y around +0xB4 by the description's +0x104. The game's buffered draw never moves on from the list's first node, so every piece it writes is the first particle's; ours does the same. Neither draw grows the box, which starts at the point +0x194. With `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_THIRD=1` in compare mode the forcing of E10d ends with the draws, on the first effect of its pair after the emit has cut the pieces: its materials' batches made (as `SC4EffectC::SetMaterials`), a made-up list of five particles, the description's lists of one or several values by turn, its turn about y in one turn of two and the blocks' bytes by turn; each draw compared as the base class's draws are (the game's run notes its calls, ours is answered from the notes) and dry: the context is made up and none of its calls nor the batches' draws is made (`g_bDry`). The first draw whole, by its pieces, by its pieces without materials and with no particles; the second with a made-up buffer of five quads' room for five particles, thirteen (three runs), none, and with its lock answering nothing. 0 divergences in 4,792/5,461/4,366 whole-model draws, 9,584/10,922/8,732 piece draws, 4,792/5,461/4,366 empty ones and 14,376/16,383/13,098 buffered draws plus 4,792/5,461/4,366 with no lock (Big City/Making Money/Rush Hour), the class's other switches compared alongside at 0. Sixteen mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 13 reported; the three missed give the same bits on these inputs (the bytes or'd when both are one, the green rounded before its byte, the second draw's part multiplied unrounded, which is exact). Parity 12/12 with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours; the three cities' shots match main's. Not reached even forced: a count or room of 2^31 or more (the run's test is signed), a list with no values (its last index would be -1) and a particle whose batch number is past the batches (the game reads past them).

**The three callees** (2026-10-31, part E11). The ported effects code still called three of the game's functions by address; each now has a switch of its own, and with it on the ported callers call ours and the function's detour runs ours for the game's own callers. `renderer.effects.move_place` has `SC4Effect::MovePlace` (0x005AC070, cdecl, written by hand in `src/core/effects_second.h`), which the particle draws and the second class's block call with a description's list at +0x108 (0x1C-byte records): each record moves the place by the sine (FSIN, kept as the x87's ten bytes, `SdSinCos`) of the place as it was on entry along the record's second to fourth floats plus the time times its first, times its last three; the first product is added to x as it is, the other two are rounded to floats first. `renderer.effects.step_b1` has `SC4Effect::StepFlagB1` (0x005B8020, ret 4, the effect in ECX not read; `src/core/effects_life.h`), which the full step calls last for descriptions with bit 0 of their +4: the occupant manager (0x00B43D0C, slot 0x90 with 0 and -1) lists what is in the box of one (0x00A81228) each way around the particle's place, as it is, into a list of the allocator at 0x00B207E4; the first occupant of type 0x278128A0 (a building) or 0xA823821E (a prop) ends the particle (its first byte cleared). On the way the game makes an occupant filter (vtables 0x00AA0E28 and 0x00AA0E40) it never uses and a cell region of the occupant's cells (slot 0x2C, 0x004422A0) that it frees at once; ours does the same. `renderer.effects.start_named` has `SC4Effect::StartNamed` (0x005B8290, ret 8; `src/core/effects_life.h`), which starts "_water" and "_terrain" for the meeting with the ground and the starts at set ages and at birth: the description's name (+0x160) and the suffix joined (the game in a string of its own at 0x00B22D5C, kept between calls and read nowhere else), made by the effects manager (0x00B43D1C, slot 0x1C), and a made effect given the 0x38-byte block (bytes 1 and 1, two left as they were on the game's stack, the identity matrix, the place, scale 1; slot 0x20) and started (slot 0xC with 0); what was made is let go. The move and the step's end can run twice and are compared on copies (the step's end as the set-up is, `SuCompared`). The start cannot: both run dry, ours with its four calls only noted and the game's with the global effects manager swapped for a made-up one whose make notes the name and hands over a made-up effect that notes its block, start and release, once with each answer of the make (the second recorded as `.none`); then the game's start runs for real. The ages' and birth's noting detour on 0x005B8290 is the same detour. No test city reaches any of the three; with `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_CALLEES=1` in compare mode each switch in compare mode is compared before every running effect's draw: the move with the description's own list or made-up lists of one or three records (one with a NaN, one with a sum out of FSIN's range, one empty) on copies of three places at three times; the start with eight suffixes (the game's "_water" and "_terrain", none, one long enough to grow the string, names of no effect) at two places; the step's end on three copies of the first running particle (as it is, at its place taken to the terrain's grid through the second block, and at the block's own place). 0 divergences in 502,260/347,237/1,259,300 forced moves and as many forced starts of each answer, and 1,235,598/703,281/2,914,305 forced steps' ends of which 121,894/97,426/556,834 ended the particle (Big City/Making Money/Rush Hour); also 0 in Big City with every other `renderer.effects.*` switch ours and the starts at set ages and at birth compared and forced. Sixteen mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 15 reported: missed was every occupant taken as a building, which changes nothing here because the first occupant in each box was always a building or a prop. Parity 12/12 with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours; the three cities' shots match main's. Not reached: a box whose first listed occupant is of another type, and a failed allocation of the filter.

**The pool's three parts** (2026-10-31, part E12). The particle takes still called two of the game's functions by address, and the pool they grow is the same one the database files' records come from; each of the three now has a switch of its own (written by hand in `src/core/effects_pool.h`), and with it on the ported callers call ours and the function's detour runs ours for the game's own callers. A pool is four words: a block's size, a chunk's bytes, the chunks and the free list. `renderer.effects.grow` has `SC4Pool::Grow` (0x005A61B0, renamed from `SC4ParticlePool::Grow`; 16 call sites, the particle pools 0x00B0A67C, 0x00B0A5C0 and 0x00B0A594 among them): a chunk of the chunk's bytes plus 8 from the C runtime's malloc (0x009F066E, its answer not checked), its first word linked to the chunks; from its fifth byte as many blocks as fit, each linked to the next and the last (by unsigned division, minus one, times the size) to none, and the free list the first; the blocks' own bytes and the chunk's tail are left as malloc gave them. `renderer.effects.allocate` has `SC4Pool::Allocate` (0x005A6290, 33 call sites, all in the database files' record code; the ported callers in `packed_file_3.cpp` and `save_file_2.cpp` reach it by its address, so through the detour): the first block of the list, after the grow when it is empty. `renderer.effects.clear` has `SC4Particle::Clear` (0x005B9DF0, called by the three classes' takes; answers the particle): +0xC, +0x10 and +0x14 get the float at 0x00B22E20 and then the first 0x1C bytes, those three with them, are zeroed. The clear is compared on a copy and the allocate on the pool (ours, the pool put back, the game's; with an empty list the game's first and its grow replayed for ours, recorded as `.grown`). The grow cannot run twice: the game's runs for real and ours runs on its chunk with the pool put back; so that bytes neither writes still count, a block of the chunk's size is first taken from malloc, filled with 0xA5 and given back, and when the game's grow gets it back ours runs on the chunk filled again with 0xA5 and the chunk is compared up to the end of its last block; otherwise ours runs on what the game's left and the whole chunk is compared (`.reused`). The test cities reach all three: 0 divergences in 22,493/21,272/16,780 allocates, 134,241/104,229/112,699 clears and 7/6/8 grows (Big City/Making Money/Rush Hour, all of the grows on the filled chunk); never an allocate from an empty list. With `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_POOL=1` in compare mode each switch in compare mode is compared before every running effect's draw: the grow on made-up pools of blocks of 4 to 0x54 bytes, chunks of one to 64 blocks and a tail of none, one byte or a block less one (the chunk given back after); the allocate on a made-up pool with a list of three blocks taken from the first, second or last, or none; the clear on copies of the first running particle as it is and filled with 0xA5, 0xFF and counting bytes. 0 divergences in 371,092/151,816/1,579,275 forced grows on the filled chunk and 1,015,777/424,553/1,809,108 on the reused one, 1,040,152/432,277/2,541,288 forced allocates and 346,717/144,092/847,095 from an empty list, and 1,386,869/576,369/3,388,383 forced clears. Fourteen mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 13 reported: missed were the three floats written after the zeroing instead of before, which gives the same bytes because the float at 0x00B22E20 is zero. Parity 12/12 with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours (Big City d1095 differed in the first run of the twelve and was the same when run again, as in part E10d). The shots with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours match main's in Making Money and Rush Hour 20 of 20 against both of main's runs, and in Big City 20 of 20 against two of three runs of main; against the third the smoke at zoom 1 (z1-r3) is at 1.20% and 1.31% against the 1.2% allowed, where main's runs differ from each other there by 1.02% to 1.08%. Not reached even forced: a pool whose blocks do not fit its chunk at all (the game's would write before the chunk) and a failed malloc (neither checks it); in the test cities, an allocate from an empty list (reached only forced).

**The index batch's three parts** (2026-10-31, part E13). The particle draws and the third class's draws still called three of the batch's functions by address; each now has a switch of its own (written by hand in `src/core/effects_index_batch.h`), and with it on the ported draws call ours and the function's detour runs ours for the game's own callers (the base class's draws 0x005B3DA0 and 0x005B4B90 and the third class's 0x005A6990 and 0x005A77F0). A batch is 0x40 bytes: +8 the vertices of a quad, +0xC its indices, +0x14 to +0x1C a vector of bytes (the vertices, +0x20 bytes each) and +0x24 their count, +0x2C to +0x34 a vector of 16-bit indices and +0x38 their count, and at +0x28 and +0x3C two fill functions (cdecl: the start, from, to). `renderer.effects.batch_clear` has `SC4IndexBatch::Clear` (0x005EC4A0, answers 0): both counts zeroed. `renderer.effects.batch_add_quads` has `SC4IndexBatch::AddQuads` (0x005EC850, ret 4): the count goes on by the quads times +0xC; when the indices' size (their bytes shifted right as a signed word; the capacity is not looked at) is below the new count, they are resized to it with a zero (`SC4Uint16Vector::Resize`, 0x00465710, still the game's by its address) and, when the batch has one, the fill at +0x3C called with the new start, the old size and the new count; answers the start plus twice the old count. `renderer.effects.batch_add_vertices` has `SC4IndexBatch::AddVertices` (0x005EC8C0, ret 4): the same for the vertices, in bytes (the new count times the stride +0x20), resized with a zero byte (`SC4ByteVector::Resize2`, 0x004610A0, still the game's) and the fill at +0x28 called with the new start, the old count and the new count; answers the start plus the stride times the old count. The batch's draw (0x005EC5D0) was left the game's here; it is ours since part E14 (below). The clear is compared on a copy. The adds may take memory, so they do not run twice: the game's runs for real with the batch's fill swapped for one that notes its arguments and calls it, and ours runs on a copy of the batch as it was, its resize noted and answered with the vector's words as the game's left them and its fill only noted; compared are the 0x40 bytes, the answer, whether and to what size each resized (with no fill, also the value against the game's first new element; with one the fill writes over it) and the fills' arguments; an add that resized is recorded as `.grown`. As the test cities run (buffered draws only): 0 divergences in 268,087/207,795/303,969 clears and as many adds of quads, 565/1,338/2,579 of them grown (Big City/Making Money/Rush Hour); the add of vertices is called only by the plain draws, which these cities do not reach, so Big City also ran with `OPENSC4_TEST_RENDER=UseVertexBuffers=0`: 0 in 489,857 adds of vertices and as many of quads, 568 of each grown. Also 0 in Big City with every other `renderer.effects.*` switch ours (215,281 clears and adds of quads through the ported draws). With `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_BATCH=1` in compare mode each switch in compare mode is compared before every running effect's draw: the clear on a copy of the effect's own batch and on batches filled with 0xA5, 0xFF and counting bytes; the adds on a made-up batch of 4, 3 or 1 vertices of 0x20, 0x18 or 0x1C bytes and 6, 4 or 3 indices to a quad, counts of 0, 5 or 0x600, a made-up fill or none, adding 0, 1, 2, 7 or 64 quads three times (to empty vectors, which takes memory; after the vectors are cut to half their size, which grows them in place; and after the counts are zeroed), the vectors' memory given back after (`SC4::Free`, 0x0090CF63). 0 divergences in 1,086,503/1,157,439/1,879,702 forced clears, 772,628/823,053/1,336,666 forced adds of quads and 2,486,881/2,649,264/4,302,440 grown, and 820,919/874,497/1,420,210 forced adds of vertices and 2,438,590/2,597,820/4,218,896 grown. Fourteen mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 14 reported; the index fill's from and the fill skipped were also reported unforced, so the game's batches have an index fill. Parity 12/12 with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours (Big City d0365 differed in the first run of the twelve and was the same when run again alone, with every switch ours and with none). The shots with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours match main's 20 of 20 in all three cities (in Big City against two of three runs of main; the third differs at zoom 1 (z1-r1 to z1-r3, 3.4% to 8.1%) from the other two runs of main as much as from ours). Not reached even forced: a size of 2^31 bytes or more (the indices' size is shifted as a signed word) and counts whose bytes pass 2^32; unforced, a batch of vertices with a fill (whether the game's have one is not known).

**The index batch's draw** (2026-10-31, part E14). `renderer.effects.batch_draw` has `SC4IndexBatch::Draw` (0x005EC5D0, ret 4, 75 instructions), written by hand in `src/core/effects_index_batch.h`: the ported draws call ours when it is on, and for the game's own callers (the plain draw at 0x005B4B03 and the third class's at 0x005A77BE; the buffered draws do not draw through the batch) it runs through the detour the particle draws' comparisons already had of it, which notes it as their callee while they note and otherwise goes by this switch. The draw hands the render context the vertices (`SC4RenderContext::SetVertexArray`, 0x007D2970: the format +0x10, the vertex count +0x24, the vertices +0x14). With an index count (+0x38, tested for zero), while the count is above 0x600 (unsigned), it draws a run of 0x600 indices from the end (`SC4RenderContext::DrawElements`, 0x007D29F0: the primitive +4, 0x600, the indices +0x2C plus the count less 0x600 as 16-bit words) and lowers the count by 0x600, then draws the rest from the first index. With no indices it does the same on the vertex count with runs of vertices (0x007FC2D0, newly named `SC4RenderContext::DrawVertexRange`: the primitive, the count less 0x600, 0x600) and the rest from the first vertex (the primitive, 0, the count). The counts are read from the batch again after each call and left at the last run's, so the draw changes the batch. Compare mode runs the game's on the batch with the context's three noted by detours of their own (compare mode only) and passed on, and ours on a copy of the batch as it was with the three only noted; compared are the calls (which, on what, with what, in order) and the batch's 0x40 bytes after. As the test cities run, the draw is not reached (only the buffered draws run), so Big City also ran with `OPENSC4_TEST_RENDER=UseVertexBuffers=0`: 0 divergences in 522,404 draws; with every other `renderer.effects.*` switch ours (through the ported draws) 0 in 417,826; and with `renderer.effects.draw_plain` compared and the draw ours (the shared detour noting it and running ours) 0 in 358,827 plain draws. With `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_BATCH=1` in compare mode it is also compared before every running effect's draw on a made-up batch with a made-up context whose calls are only noted: a primitive of 0 to 6, the indices at 0, near the top of memory or at counting bytes, counts of 0, 1, 2, 0x5FF, 0x600, 0x601, 0xBFF, 0xC00, 0xC01, 0x1801 or 0x3000 vertices and (every other turn) indices, and one turn in 4,999 with 0x80000001 indices or vertices (above 0x600 only when unsigned). 0 divergences in 579,950/525,377/2,027,107 forced draws (Big City/Making Money/Rush Hour). Fourteen mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 14 reported; the two signed loops only by the turns with 0x80000001 (124 and 121 divergences). Parity 12/12 with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours. The shots with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours match main 20 of 20 against two runs of main in all three cities (they draw through the buffer, not the batch), and in Big City with `UseVertexBuffers=0` also 20 of 20 against three runs of main in a second run of ours. The first run of ours was 19 of 20 against the first two runs of main (z1-r3, smoke at 1.2% and 1.4%) and 20 of 20 against the third. Not known: whether a real batch ever has more than 0x600 indices or vertices.

To reach it in a test: `OPENSC4_TEST_RENDER=UseVertexBuffers=0`. The effects manager hands an effect the dynamic buffer only while that render property (bool 25, the byte at +0x33C of the properties) is set; without the buffer every effect of the base class draws with 0x005B3DA0. 2026-10-11, 30 days on each tutorial city with our driver (the draws are only captured through it) and `renderer.effects.particles=compare`: 0 divergences in 24,943 (Big City), 21,503 (Making Money) and 25,378 calls (Rush Hour), each with a draw compared; Big City also ran with it ours. The self-check and the camera workout were not run with this setting. 2026-10-23, with this setting, the camera workout, clouds and water effects on and the switches as for the buffered draw (with `renderer.effects.draw_plain=compare`): 0 divergences in 184,790, 102,637 and 172,375 calls (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour). Not reached then: an effect without a texture, the turn about the y axis and the axes along the velocity. The opacity test turned the other way (a mistake put in on purpose) was reported.

**An effect that is not drawn** (the base class's slot 0x4C, 0x005B6390: no render context, because it is out of view, or no particle) still keeps the box around its particles (+0x1A0 to +0x1B4; presumably what the manager's test for what is in view asks for, not checked): the box is started anew and each particle's position (+0xC), taken through the effect's matrix (+0xC8), widens it, as the draws do.

`renderer.effects.box` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_life.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). The box starts empty (its low corner one, its high corner zero) and the first particle makes it; a corner keeps its value only while it is strictly beyond the point, so a NaN takes the point; the x the game compares is the double it keeps on the x87 stack, the one it stores rounded. When the box is not empty at the end it grows on every side by the first float of the description's list at +0xAC. It writes only the box, so compare mode runs ours and the game's on the same effect. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all seven of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 968,499, 1,319,080 and 1,565,906 calls. A mistake put in on purpose in the growth was reported. The second class's own version (0x005AE6E0) is `renderer.effects.notdrawn_b` (below).

**The pull on an effect's particles from outside** (0x005B6C40; the effect's draw calls it when the description lists any, +0x1A4 to +0x1A8, and its +0x1B0 is above 0). For descriptions with flag 0x08000000 it only copies three floats of the effect (+0xB4) to +0x178. Else it takes the middle of the effect's box and, when the description names a kind at +0x1B4, asks a service of the game (the object at 0x00B43CEC: its slot 0xBC gives an object whose slot 0x58 is asked with a query object built on the stack, 0x005B2A40, and answers three vectors), scales what comes back (by the value at 0x00B0A664 over a length), and from that and the description's values works out the velocity at +0x16C that the draw adds to every particle's (the arithmetic is in the translation and has not been written out here).

`renderer.effects.pull` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_life.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22); the service and the query object (0x005B2A40, freed by 0x005E8C40) are the game's, the game's 0x6C-byte stack frame kept as one buffer so the service writes where it does in the game. In words: with a kind the vector the service answers is made one long and turned back, its negated length the strength and the measure zero; without one the direction is the flat way from the box's middle to +0x178 (one long when it has a length, that length the measure, +0x184 the strength). A strength of zero clears the velocity (+0x16C). Else the measure over the description's +0x1B0, held to zero to one, picks a value from the description's list at +0x1A4 (between two neighbours, the index by the float trick of 0x00AA0CF8), times the strength; for flag 0x04000000 that is kept at +0x1D0 and the velocity cleared, else the velocity is the direction taken through the second block (by columns, over its scale) times it. It writes only the effect, so compare mode runs ours and the game's on the same effect, asking the service twice. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all seven of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 650,409, 254,897 and 531,135 calls. A count of its branches showed every call in Big City taking the flag 0x04000000 path; a mistake there was reported, and mistakes in the copy (flag 0x08000000) and in the full velocity went unseen.

**An effect's wandering** (0x005B2730; the last thing the effect's draw does for descriptions with flag 0x800000, unless the effect has just ended). A timer at +0x148 is lowered by the step. Each time it has run out it gets a random time more, between two values of the description (+0x174), and the effect's own velocity (+0x150 to +0x158) is turned. As far as the listing was read: when the velocity has no length to speak of, a random direction is taken first (0x0080B680); then its heading (the x87 arctangent) is changed by random amounts and the velocity is put together again from sines and cosines. Every random number comes from the generator at 0x00B22DF8.

`renderer.effects.wander` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_life.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). The timer is checked on the unrounded difference; when the velocity's square in the plane is below the double at 0x00A9F190 a random heading comes first (0x0080B680). With a direction (+0x15C, the vector at +0x160) the heading is turned both ways by a random amount up to +0x184, plus +0x188, times the float at 0x00AA0D04, and of the two velocities the one further along the direction is taken if it is further along than the old one, else the velocity is left; without one the heading is turned by a random amount within +0x184 plus the double at 0x00A95620 and on by +0x188 the same way. The arctangent and the sines and cosines (multiplied by the speed before they are stored) are the x87's own instructions. It writes the effect and moves the generator on, so compare mode runs ours and the game's on the same effect and generator. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all seven of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 103,527, 103,206 and 112,899 calls. A mistake put in on purpose in the timer was reported.

**The start edge and the starts at set ages** (0x005B7030 and 0x005B8410, the two extra steps of the effect's draw). 0x005B7030 (ret 4, with a word that both callers give as 0: the draw for flag 0x2000 and the effect's taking of a description for flags 0x1000 and 0x2000) puts the limits of where particles start (+0x200 to +0x214) on one edge of the map, whose size the effect keeps at +0x1F4 and +0x1F8. For flag 0x2000 the word grows by how the vector at +0x1E8's +8 stands: its first float above its third, 3 when that first one is above zero, else 2; otherwise 0 when the third is above zero, else 1 (a NaN counts as not above). Then by the word (unsigned, the jump table at 0x005B715C): 0 the first axis from zero to +0x1F4 at the third axis's zero; 1 the first at +0x1F4, the third from zero to +0x1F8; 2 the first from zero to +0x1F4 at the third's +0x1F8; 3 the first at zero, the third from zero to +0x1F8; any other word leaves them. The second axis is not touched. 0x005B8410 (no arguments) starts effects where particles reach set ages: the description's table at +0x1CC holds entries of 16 bytes, a suffix (the address of a string) first and an age at +0xC. For each running particle of the list at +0x14 (the table's count worked out anew for each) and each entry whose age the particle's (+4) has not passed and reaches within the step (its age less the entry's at most zero, and that plus the step at +0x108 above zero, at double precision), 0x005B8290 starts the effect named by the description's name and the suffix at the particle's place (+0xC). The emitters' 0x005B84C0 walks the same table for the entries of age zero (below, "The starts at birth").

`renderer.effects.start_edge` and `renderer.effects.age_starts` replace them, written by hand in `src/core/effects_life.h` (2026-10-24, `SetStartEdge` and `StartAtAges`; off by default). The start edge writes only the effect and is compared as the pull (`SuCompared`). The starts at set ages start other effects, which cannot run twice: ours runs first with each start only noted (the effect, the suffix, the place's address and its three floats), then the game's with 0x005B8290's calls noted at its entry by a detour of compare mode and passed on; the notes must be the same and ours must leave the effect as it was. While an effect's draw is compared, both are recorded by these switches' detours (they replaced the draw's own two). No description in the test cities has flag 0x2000 or 0x80000000, so neither is called there. `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_STEPS=1` (read only by these two switches in compare mode) runs both on every running effect at the start of its draw, through the draw's detour, and puts back what they change. The start edge takes one choice per draw in turn (96 in all) of the description's flag 0x2000 (set for the call or cleared), the vector at +0x1E8 (the effect's own or one of seven made up: the first float above the third and above zero, above the third and below zero, above the third and at zero, below the third with the third above zero, below the third with both below zero, equal, a NaN) and the word (0 to 4 or -1); the description's word and the effect are put back after. The starts at set ages get the description's table swapped for eight made-up entries whose ages lie around the first running particle's (its age, half a step and a step on, the floats just below and above it, zero, a thousand on, a NaN) with suffixes of their own; the game's run only notes those starts, never passes them on. They are recorded as `effects.start_edge.forced` and `effects.age_starts.forced`. 2026-10-24, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on, sound off, forcing on: 0 divergences in 1,274,595, 309,595 and 497,524 start edges and 1,037,633, 236,900 and 398,030 starts at set ages (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour); a side run on Big City with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours, `renderer.effects.draw_effect` compared and the two forced: 0 divergences in 460,792 draws, 460,792 start edges and 397,719 starts at set ages. Mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 12 of 14 reported: the first float against the third taken as at least, each of the two comparisons with zero taken as at least, word 2's third-axis start cleared, word 0's third-axis end set, the flag ignored, words above 3 taken as 3, the age left taken as below zero only, the reach taken as at least zero, the step left out, the suffix read from +4, the first entry only. Unseen: word 1's third-axis end from +0x1F4 (the test cities' maps are square, both 2,048) and the running check left out (every particle in the list runs when the draw starts: the draw unlinks the ones that end). With every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours and forcing off: parity 12 of 12 for 7 days (two Rush Hour saves, d0000 and d0090, differed on the first run with the game alone giving more than one outcome, and were the same on a rerun, as on main), screenshots 20 of 20 in the three cities against two runs of main.

**The starts at birth** (0x005B84C0, ret 4, with a particle; class name ours `SC4Effect::StartAtBirth`, was `SpawnFromParticle`). The emitters' take-and-step calls it for descriptions with flag 0x80000000 with a particle just taken and stepped that still runs, before it joins the list. For a particle that runs (its first byte), the description's table at +0x1CC (above; its count worked out once) is walked from its first entry while the entry's age (+0xC) equals the zero at 0x00A81054 as floats (so -0 starts too, and a NaN ends the walk), and 0x005B8290 starts the effect named by the description's name and the entry's suffix at the particle's place (+0xC). The first entry of another age ends the walk, so only the entries of age zero at the table's head are started.

`renderer.effects.birth_starts` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_life.h` (2026-10-25, `StartAtBirth`; off by default) and compared as the starts at set ages: ours with each start noted, then the game's with 0x005B8290's calls noted at its entry. The pool's comparisons and an effect's draw's record it through this switch's detour (it had a recording detour of its own before). No test city reaches it. With `OPENSC4_EFFECT_FORCE_STEPS=1` its compare mode runs it on every running effect at the start of its draw on the first running particle and on a copy of it that does not run, with the description's table swapped in turn for one of six made up (eight zeros; zero, -0, the smallest float, then zeros; a NaN first; one first; seven zeros and -1; no entries), the game's starts only noted, as `effects.birth_starts.forced`. 2026-10-25, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on, sound off, forcing on: 0 divergences in 1,502,638, 2,121,366 and 6,037,016 calls (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour); a side run on Big City with every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours, `renderer.effects.draw_effect` compared and the starts at birth forced: 0 divergences in 590,330 draws and 959,424 starts at birth. Mistakes put in on purpose (Big City, forced), 8 of 8 reported: the running check left out, a later zero started after another age, -0 taken as another age, a NaN taken as zero, the place read from the particle's start, the suffix read from +4, the first entry only, one entry short. With every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours and forcing off: parity 12 of 12 for 7 days, screenshots 20 of 20 in the three cities against two runs of main.

**Whether an effect is over** (0x005B9D40, on the sub-object at the effect's +4; the last question of the effect's draw). With a particle left (+0x14) it is not. With none, an effect marked at +0xC has its marks set anew through its slot 0x20 (0x005B9E70, with two zeros), and one marked only at +0xD lets go of what it holds (slot 0x14: 0x005B2440, or 0x005AC140 for the second class) and is released (slot 8); in both cases the answer is true.

`renderer.effects.finish` replaces it, written by hand from its thirty instructions. It may end with the effect released, so it is compared by record and replay, with the three functions it calls recorded. The comparison found a flaw in the recording first: a function that is itself reached through a recording detour started one level deep, and the calls it made were not noted; the record now starts at the function compared. 2026-10-11, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on: 0 divergences (1,576,602, 1,870,273 and 1,737,769 calls; in one Big City run 1,477 of them ended an effect through slot 0x20); the comparisons of the count and stream emitters, run again after that change, also 0. With all fifteen replacements of this section on, Big City ran the camera workout, and its screenshots with clouds off match two runs without them in 20 and 20 of 20.

**The particle pool.** Particles are 0x44-byte records kept on a free list (0x00B0A688, linked through each one's first four bytes) of a pool at 0x00B0A67C. Three slots of the base effect class's sub-object use it. Taking a particle (slot 0x3C, 0x005B7C20) takes the first of the list, after the pool has grown when the list is empty (0x005A61B0), clears it (0x005B9DF0), sets six of its floats to zero and has the effect start it (0x005B7580, 477 instructions that give it its first values from the effect and its description). Adding one (slot 0x2C, 0x005B9F80) puts a new 12-byte node for it at the end of the effect's list and raises the count. Giving one back (slot 0x40, 0x005B2240) puts it at the head of the free list.

`renderer.effects.pool` replaces the three, written by hand; growing the pool and clearing a particle (since part E12 `renderer.effects.grow` and `renderer.effects.clear`, below) and starting it were still the game's, as were the second class's own take and give back (0x005AEF20, 0x005AF390), which have their own switches since (`renderer.effects.pool_b`, `renderer.effects.giveback_b`). Each has its own comparison. The take: the game's runs for real, and a detour on the start of the particle keeps the particle and the free list as they were at that point; the free list and the particle are put back as before, ours runs up to the same point, and the two are compared (a take that would grow the pool is not compared). The add: ours adds its node, the shape of the list's end is noted, the node is taken off and freed, and the game's adds its own. The give back: both from the same free list. 2026-10-11, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on: 0 divergences (takes 1,266,769, 1,065,139 and 1,384,219; adds 591,809, 842,003 and 1,091,556; give backs as the takes). With all sixteen replacements of this section on, Big City's screenshots with clouds off match two runs without them in 19 and 20 of 20 (the miss the smoke at zoom 1, 1.28% against 1.2%), and the camera workout ran to its end in four runs of five; the other ended in the game at 0x0096DA20, the rare crash of the unmodified game that [crashes.md](crashes.md) describes.

**Stopping and ending an effect.** Slot 4 of an effect (0x005B21E0) keeps a value at +0x18C and tells the sub-object to go on when it is 0 and to end otherwise; the second class's (0x005AC190) first passes the value to the object at its +0x278, when it has one and nothing at +0x18. The sub-object's routine (0x005B9E70, slot 0x20, with two flags) does nothing for an effect whose byte +5 is clear. Told to go on, it keeps the two flags at +0xC and +0xD (and calls its slot 4 when both are set). Told to end, it clears the bytes +5 and +0xD, gives every particle of the list back to the pool, frees the nodes and leaves the list empty.

`renderer.effects.ending` replaces the three, written by hand, compared by record and replay as the effect's draw. A stop setter's comparison holds the sub-object's routine as one recorded call, so that routine was also compared on its own with the stop setters ours (a switch for that one test, not kept). 2026-10-11, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (stop setters 33,074, 22,126 and 12,316 calls, the routine on its own 5,295, 4,658 and 3,455; with the stop setters ours, Big City and Rush Hour: 4,334 and 4,384 calls told to go on, 22,621 and 17,430 told to end). With the seventeen replacements of this section on, Big City's screenshots with clouds off match both runs without them in 20 of 20, and the camera workout ran to its end. Letting go of what an effect holds (0x005B2440, 0x005AC140) is still the game's: no test city calls either.

**A particle's start.** Taking a particle from the pool ends in 0x005B7580 (477 instructions), which gives it its first values: its first byte marks it as running, its age (+4) is 0, its life (+8) is a random time between two values of the description, and its position, velocity and the other fields up to +0x40 come from the effect, its description and random numbers of the generator at 0x00B22DF8 (one arctangent, three square roots). For descriptions with a flag of the mask at 0x00B22E18 it then calls 0x005B71E0, which asks the terrain for the height under the particle and puts it on the ground or ends it at once; that one is still the game's.

`renderer.effects.start` replaces the start, written by hand in `src/core/effects_setup.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). The turn for a description whose byte +0xD5 is 1 is taken with the x87's own instructions (FPATAN gives a 64-bit result whatever the precision control). It writes only the particle and draws from the generator, so compare mode runs ours, keeps the particle, the effect and the generator it left, puts all three back and runs the game's (`SuCompared`, shared with the placing on the ground and the effect's set-up). 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all nine of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 239,553, 209,924 and 108,509 starts. Mistakes put in on purpose: the turn was reported; the pull towards the description's centre (+0xF0) and the move along the axes (flag 0x20) went unseen, no description in the test cities having them.

**A new particle put on the ground.** 0x005B71E0 (the start calls it for descriptions with a flag of the mask at 0x00B22E18, which in the test cities is nearly every start) takes the particle's position to the terrain's grid with the effect's matrix (+0xC8) and ends the particle at once (its first byte cleared, the answer false) when that is off the map (+0x1F4, +0x1F8), when the height the terrain answers there (the object at +0x1F0, slot 0x34) less the effect's own (+0x1FC) is outside two values of the description (+0x158, +0x15C; flag 0x10000), or when the cell is of the wrong kind (flags of the mask at 0x00B22E1C and flag 0x20000; asked of the object at 0x00B43CEC). Otherwise, for flag 0x4000, the particle's position is set from that height (with flag 0x8000 not below the effect's own; without 0x4000 but with 0x8000 the effect's own) plus a random amount between the effect's +0x204 and +0x210, taken back through the matrix; the answer is true.

`renderer.effects.ground` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_setup.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22), compared as the start (the comparison also compares the answers). 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all nine of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 469,710, 410,444 and 206,064 placings (each start's placing is compared inside ours' and inside the game's run of the start). A mistake in the height was reported; one in the cell's kind (flags of the mask at 0x00B22E1C) went unseen, no description in the test cities asking for it.

**The other step.** For descriptions with a flag of the mask at 0x00B22E14 the particle's step jumps to 0x005B9790, and in the test cities most steps do. The age and the end are as in the plain step; then the step is done in parts of at most the float at 0x00AA0CF4.
