Each part moves the particle by its velocity (descriptions with flag 0x10000000 through 0x005B6600 instead), adds the effect's velocity change (+0x13C) and the description's to the velocity, takes the position to the terrain's grid, and ends the particle when it is off the map (flag 0x800) or, on the map, when 0x005B8DC0 answers false (flags 0x100 and 0x400: the ground under the particle, a random number against the description's +0x154, and perhaps another effect started there). Last, descriptions with bit 0 of their second word of flags go to 0x005B8020. The three functions it calls are still the game's.

`renderer.effects.step_full` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_life.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22); the move along a path (0x005B6600), the meeting with the ground (0x005B8DC0, `renderer.effects.hit` below) and 0x005B8020 are called as they are. Read closer than before: the velocity change of each part is the effect's (+0x13C); for flag 0x8000000 the velocity is drawn towards the effect's +0x178 by a value of the description's list at +0x1A4 at the distance over +0x1B0; the damping is by the step still to do (not the part) times the description's +0x100; the answer is the particle's first byte. Its callees write the particle and the effect and draw random numbers, all of which can be done twice, so compare mode runs ours and the game's on the same particle, effect and generator; a step that could start another effect (an effect with +0x218 or +0x219, a description with bit 0 of its +4) is not compared, and none was in the test cities. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all seven of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 9,108,337, 8,817,895 and 18,277,727 steps. A mistake put in on purpose in the damping was reported.

**A particle meeting the water or the ground.** The other step calls 0x005B8DC0 (714 instructions, to 0x005B96CE) in every part, for descriptions with flag 0x100 or 0x400, with the particle's place on the terrain's grid; false ends the particle. A particle at or below the water's height (the effect's +0x1FC) ends by a random number (0x0091372E on the generator at 0x00B22DF8) against the description's +0x154, and an effect with its +0x219 set starts another effect there, named by its own name and "_water" (0x005B8290). Otherwise the terrain is asked for the height and the slope under the particle (the interface 0x0A5EAA1A of the service at 0x00B43D1C, and the object at the effect's +0x1F0): a particle that has reached the ground bounces (its velocity turned about the ground's normal and scaled by values of the description), slides, stays where it is or ends (an effect with +0x218 set starts "_terrain" there, and a second random number may let it live), and some descriptions scale the effect's own velocity (+0x150).

`renderer.effects.hit` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_life.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). Read closer: in the water the particle ends when the description's +0x154 is one, or not zero and at least the random number; the terrain's interface is asked of the service (slot 0, held and let go at once, let go again before each answer). For flag 0x400 a point ahead of the particle (along its velocity by +0x144) is asked its height over the ground (the interface's slot 0x18): below +0x14C the particle ends, at or above +0x13C nothing happens, between, the ground's normal (slot 0x20) times the square of how far below +0x13C it is pushes the particle's velocity, or, with flag 0x800000, the effect's drift (+0x150), kept to its old length. Else with +0x138 zero the particle is put on the ground and slides; with +0x138 the particle below the height of slot 0x4C may start "_terrain" (+0x218), ends by the chance at +0x150 or is put on the ground and bounces by one plus +0x138 about the normal. It writes the particle and the effect, draws random numbers and asks the terrain, all of which can be done twice; the effects it may start cannot, so an effect with +0x218 or +0x219 set is not compared (none was in the test cities). Compare mode runs ours and the game's on the same particle, effect and generator. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all seven of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 18,254,262, 17,608,030 and 36,495,436 calls. A count of its branches in Big City reached the water's end, the point ahead at or above +0x13C, the push of flag 0x400, the particle left above the height of slot 0x4C, the chance at +0x150 and the bounce; mistakes put in on purpose in the water's end, the push, the chance and the bounce: all four reported (in 65, 11,781, 11,636 and 8 calls). Not reached: no interface, the point ahead off the map, the end below +0x14C, the drift of flag 0x800000 and the slide (+0x138 zero).

**The second class's take.** The second effect class (vtable 0x00AA0C58) keeps particles of 0x54 bytes in a pool of its own (0x00B0A5C0, free list 0x00B0A5CC). Its take (0x005AEF20) clears the first of the list (0x005ADCB0) and starts it as any particle. A particle that is running after that gets the two words at +0x48 zeroed, an angle at +0x50 (the arctangent of two values of the effect's matrix times the float at 0x00B0A5A4, or -999 for kinds 2 to 4 of the description's +0xD5) and, when the description names one (+0x160), an effect of its own at +0x44: the effects manager (0x00B43D1C, slot 0x1C) makes it by that name, its slot 0x34 is called with 0xD and the effect's +0x270 when the effect's byte +0x26F is set, and its slot 0xC with the effect's +0x188. So a particle of this class carries a whole effect.

`renderer.effects.pool_b` replaces the take, written by hand in `src/core/effects_second.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). The clear (0x005ADCB0) is written out (the particle's own clear 0x005B9DF0, six floats from 0x00B22D4C, +0x44 zeroed); the turn is the x87's FPATAN times 1/2pi stored as a float; the effect's name is the description's name joined with "_slave" (0x0041E270, freed after). An effect the particle already held would be let go first, but the clear always leaves +0x44 empty. Its comparison is the notes' engine's, as the holders', the game's first: the effects manager, each particle's own effect, the effect the manager makes and the object at the effect's +0x278 get copies of their tables of functions whose slots note each call (on the effect's thread, outside other notes), and the game's runs with the calls passed on; the effect, the class's free list, the generator and the particles are put back and ours runs with every such call answered from the notes; the calls and those bytes must be the same, and what the game's left is put back (the particle's start runs in both, from the same particle and generator); a take that would grow the pool is not compared. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, the four of the second class compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 604, 2,843 and 162 takes, of which 351 of the first 512, 70 of the first 2,048 and 56 of the first 128 made an effect of their own, each followed by its slot 0xC. Not reached: slot 0x34 (no effect with +0x26F set). A mistake put in on purpose in the value slot 0xC is given was reported.

**The fourth list's step.** After the sorted effects the manager's draw calls slot 0x24 of every object of its fourth list (class 0x00A9EE78, 0x00582920; about 250 a frame in Big City). It draws nothing itself. An object without a handle (+0x70 is -1) does nothing. Its time (+0x114) grows by the step, and the time times +0x118 is where it is in its life: a looping one (+0x15 is 1) wraps, one past its end that is of kind 2 ends itself (its slot 4 with 0) and any other stays at the end. The description's curves (vectors of floats from +0x34 and one of positions at +0x40) are then read at that point and given to the owner (+0x120) for the handle: by the flags at +0xC only an opacity (slot 0x44), or a block of nine values (slot 0x34) and the opacity, and last a position (slot 0x48). The name "decal" for the class is ours, from what the values look like; what the owner is has not been looked at.

`renderer.effects.decal_step` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_draw.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, `renderer.effects.decal`, removed 2026-10-22). Read closer: `t` is the time, unrounded, times +0x118; a looping one keeps what is past the whole number (rounded down by 1.5 times 2^23, 0x00A9EE6C, less 0.5, 0x00A84D2C, one up when exactly that), any other past the double at 0x00A80AB0 stays at 1 or, of kind 2, ends itself. A curve of one value is that value; else the place is the last index times `t`, rounded down the same way, and past zero (0x00A81054) the value is interpolated towards the next. The block's width and depth are, with flag 8, +0x100 times +0x64 and that times the curve at +0x4C's value, else two ones; the position needs more than one of the 12-byte points, the part rounded to a float, the second and third axes' first products rounded. Compared as before: a stand-in owner and a copy of the decal's table with slot 4 replaced note each call, ours runs with them only noted, the decal is put back, the game's runs with them noted and passed on, the notes and the decals must be the same. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (the four of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 921,312, 453,426 and 1,343,062 steps (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour). Not reached: a decal without a handle, one past its end (kind 2 or not), flag 8, more than one point and a curve read exactly on a point; no looping decal passed its first loop. Mistakes put in on purpose: the block's turn without +0xF0 was reported, a looping decal not wrapped was not (none passed its first loop).

**A decal's update** (0x00582600, slot 3 of the decal's table, with two transforms) is described with its small functions below. `renderer.effects.decal_update` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_draw.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, part of `renderer.effects.decal_parts`, removed 2026-10-22). With the first transform alone its values are taken, with the second alone (or neither) the second's, with both the first's place and rows taken through the second's (the sums unrounded, the products of float values exact) and its scale times the second's; the turn is the x87's own arctangent (FPATAN, 64 bits) of the second row's first value, negated, over the first's, times the float at 0x00B0A210. The owner's calls (with the decal's flag 0x40 and a handle) read the curves at the zero of 0x00A81054 and pass a block whose first word, and what follows the fourth, the game leaves as its stack held them; ours zero. It only writes the decal, so compare mode runs the game's, puts the decal back and runs ours (`DcDecalCompared`); an update that would call the owner runs the game's only. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (the four of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 66,947, 44,501 and 27,595 updates (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour). Not reached: +0x110 set, the second transform with no first, the owner's calls; both transforms only in Rush Hour. Mistakes put in on purpose: the scale without +0xF8 and the composed place without the first transform's were reported, the composed turn with two of the first's values swapped was not (both were the same there).

**A decal's draw** (0x00582090 to 0x005825FB, slot 0 of the decal's table, with a word saying whether to start at the end; reached from a compound effect's start of one record, 0x005BE920, and the manager's start of a compound effect, 0x005919D0). It draws nothing either: it puts the decal on the terrain, once. A decal already shown (+8 set) does nothing, and so does one told to start at the end that is of kind 2 (+0x15); one of kind 3 starts at 1, any other at 0. Without the terrain's view (0x00602160, the terrain's slot 66) nothing more happens. A decal without an owner (+0x120) takes one of the view's four overlay managers (its slot 0x48, see "Bookkeeping" above): the number is the description's flag 4, plus 2 without its flag 0x40, held as a counted reference (0x005447B0); without one nothing more happens. The size (the curve at +0x28 times +0xEC) and the turn (the curve at +0x1C times +0xFC plus +0xF0), read at the start, and the place (+0xE4) go to the manager: with flag 0x20 AddRingDecal (slot 7) with the word at +0x10, else with a texture at +0x11C AddDecal(texture) (slot 4) with it, else AddDecal(ID) (slot 6) with the word at +0x10. The answer is the handle (+0x70), and nothing more for -1. The handle's description of nine values is asked for (DecalInfo, slot 14, the first two words zeroed first), gets the curve at +0x4C times +0x104 as its fifth, with flag 8 +0x100 times +0x64 and that times the fifth as its sixth and seventh (else two ones) and +0x68 and +0x6C as its last two, and goes back (UpdateDecalInfo, slot 13). With flag 2 the overlay's flags get 1 (slot 20). Then the opacity as the step gives it (SetOverlayAlpha, slot 17), the point of the list at +0x40 the start has reached (SetOverlayColor, slot 18; with one point that one) and the byte +0x14 (SetOverlayDrawMode, slot 19). Last the start goes to +0x114 and +8 is set. The step (above) then moves what this put there; the curves and points are read the step's way, which at 0 and 1 never interpolates.

`renderer.effects.decal_draw` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_draw.h` (2026-10-24). In compare mode the decal is given a stand-in overlay manager as its owner, and a detour of 0x00602160 (installed then only) gives the decal's draw a stand-in view whose slot 0x48 hands out that stand-in: the game's draw runs first, each call noted, made on the real view or manager and its answer kept; the decal is put back and ours runs with the calls noted and answered from the game's. DecalInfo is asked twice more in the game's run, on blocks of zero and of 0xFF bytes, so that ours is given exactly the bytes it writes. The calls (slots, words and the bytes behind their pointers) and the decal must be the same; the decal then keeps the game's. 2026-10-24, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on, sound off (the other draws of this file ours): 0 divergences in 1,082, 130 and 154 draws (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour), most from the compound effect's start of one record (89%, 66% and 57% in the probe's census) and the rest from the manager's start. In all of them every draw is a decal's first: no owner yet, told to start at the end but never of kind 2 or 3, overlay managers 1 to 3 (never 0), AddDecal(ID), curves of one value, one point and no flag 2, 8 or 0x20. Not reached as they run: a decal already shown, kind 2 or 3, no view, an owner already there or none handed out, manager 0, the ring and texture adds, a handle of -1, flags 2 and 8, curves or points of more than one. A probe build that changes the decal's input on purpose for both runs (flags 2 and 8, kind 3 with lists of three values and three points, flags 4 and 0x40 swapped, flag 0x20, kind 2, and each decal drawn again with its owner and once more after +8 is cleared) reached all of them but no view, no manager handed out, the texture add and a handle of -1: 0 divergences in 1,368 and 240 draws and 304 and 54 already shown (Big City, Rush Hour). Mistakes put in on purpose, with those inputs on Big City, 9 of 12 reported: the size without +0xEC, the manager's number, the opacity without +0x108, the draw mode from +0x15, +0x114 and +8 not set, kind 3 starting at 0, the point at 1 taken as the first and slot 20 called without flag 2. Three went unseen, presumably for the values there: the turn without +0xF0, the fifth value without +0x104 and the seventh without the fifth (+0xF0 0 and +0x104 one in those decals). Parity (every `renderer.effects.*` switch ours, muted, 7 days): 12 of 12 saves the same. Screenshots against the unmodified game: 20 of 20 in all three cities (in Big City against a third run of the game; its first two runs miss the zoom-1 smoke shots at rotations 1 and 3 against the third, and ours as they miss each other). With the manager's draw, an effect's draw and the decal's step and update in compare mode as well: 0 divergences in 12,302 and 17,523 manager frames (Big City, Rush Hour).

**The second class's step out of view.** An effect of the second class that is not drawn still steps (slot 0x4C, 0x005AE6E0): its box (+0x1A0) is started again and at the end widened by the effect's +0x274, and each particle of the list whose own effect (+0x44) is there and answers its slot 0x14 is told where it is. For that 0x005ACA30 (900 instructions, `renderer.effects.block_b` below) makes a block of two bytes and thirteen floats from the particle and the effect's matrix, turning the particle as it goes (+0x48 to +0x50); the particle's effect then gets one value of a curve of the description at the particle's age (slot 0x30 with 3) and the block (slot 0x20), or, for descriptions with flag 8 of their third word of flags, a second block with the turning left out (slot 0x1C).

`renderer.effects.notdrawn_b` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_second.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). Read closer: the block's scale is the description's list at +0xAC read at the particle's age over its life, times the particle's +0x28 and the double at 0x00AA02D8; the value of slot 0x30 is the effect's +0x1D0 times the particle's +0x34 times the list at +0xA0 read at the same point, for descriptions with flag 0x10 of their third word; the block of slot 0x1C has one on its diagonal, the first block's place, both bytes 1 and the scale one. A particle without an own effect that answers is taken through the second block as the base class's box does it, but with its x and y scaled before they are rounded to floats, and widens the box; the box grows at the end by the list at +0xAC times +0x274 times the double at 0x00AA02D8. Its comparison is the notes' engine's, as the holders', the game's first: the effects manager, each particle's own effect, the effect the manager makes and the object at the effect's +0x278 get copies of their tables of functions whose slots note each call (on the effect's thread, outside other notes), and the game's runs with the calls passed on; the effect, the class's free list, the generator and the particles are put back and ours runs with every such call answered from the notes; the calls and those bytes must be the same, and what the game's left is put back; the block runs in both. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, the four of the second class compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 261,216, 283,449 and 477,144 steps. Not reached: slot 0x1C (no description with flag 8). Mistakes put in on purpose in the value of slot 0x30 and in the x of a particle without an own effect were both reported.

**The second class's draw.** The second class draws each particle as a model (slot 0x44, 0x005ADD50, 654 instructions): the effect keeps a model instance (+0x220) and the model (+0x224). The box is kept as in the step out of view. For each particle, descriptions with flag 2 of their third word of flags first ask the game's 0x005AD890 whether it is in view. The particle's block comes from 0x005ACA30 and is scaled by a curve of the description; the instance is given the block (slot 0x28) and the particle's colour and opacity, in the block of values the instance itself gave at the start (slots 0x3C and 0x40). A model of more than one frame is set to the frame the particle's own clock (+0x48) has reached (slot 0x28): the clock runs by the effect's step, scaled by the description's +0x1DC, or by the particle's speed, and by flags of the description the frames run once, loop or stop at the last. For other models an effect with +0x26C set uses its part at +0x228 (0x00803E10 and 0x005AC200, not looked at). Then the instance is drawn (slot 0x30 with the render context), and a particle's own effect is told where it is, as out of view. Last the context's colour is reset (0x007D3290).

`renderer.effects.draw_b` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_particles_draw.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22); 0x005AD890, 0x005ACA30 and the part's two functions are still the game's. Read closer: the depth handed to 0x005AD890 is the camera's (the context's +0x24) +0x40 over its +0x58, less ((+0x44 times +0x5C plus +0x40 times +0x58) plus +0x3C times +0x54), plus the vector at +0x1E8's +0x30, rounded once. A particle's place is turned about the y axis by its height above the effect's +0xB8 times the description's +0x104 (when that is not the double at 0x00A80990) with the x87's own sine and cosine, 64 bits, the turned offsets added to the particle's own x and z. The colour list is read as the step out of view reads its lists, with the part rounded to a float; the next value's red part stays unrounded, the rest's parts are rounded. The clock's step is the frame's (+0x108), times +0x1DC when that is above zero; for flag 0x40 of the description's third word times the particle's speed, else over the model's frames (its slot 0x44's word at +2) times the float at 0x00A8FAF0. For flag 0x20 a clock that has reached the frames shows the last; else the clock loses whole loops when it has reached them and is rounded down. The instance's colour block starts as what it answers (slot 0x3C), its bit 2 set for descriptions with flag 1, its bit 1 cleared only when the opacity is one and the list at +0xA0 has one value or none. Compared by the notes' engine, as the step out of view: the instance, the model and the particles' own effects get noting tables; detours of compare mode note the check whether a particle is in view (the place and scale it leaves noted too and given to ours), the part's two functions and the colour reset; the instance's colour as it answered is noted and given to ours (the game's run sets it again for each particle). 2026-10-23, three test cities as for the buffered draw: 0 divergences in 10,893, 6,363 and 38,360 draws (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour). Not reached in the test cities: descriptions with flag 1 (the colour's bit 2), the turn about the y axis, the check whether a particle is in view, a colour list of more than one value, a model of more than one frame (the clock and frames), the part's step and the placed block for flag 8; the instance was drawn in 2,669 draws, always with the part's start. Mistakes put in on purpose: the box grown without +0x274 and the block given to the own effects moved were reported; the green times the particle's +0x40 for its +0x3C was not (presumably the same values in these cities).

**The second class's block.** Both of the class's routines get a particle's block from 0x005ACA30 (900 instructions, to 0x005AD885; the particle, a word, a scale, a pointer to three floats and the block to fill). The block is two bytes and thirteen floats: a matrix of the particle's turning and size, and its place. How the particle is turned goes by the description's kind (+0xD5): kinds 2 to 4 face the camera in one of three ways (the game's 0x0080B910, 0x0080B530 and 0x0080B830), the others turn about an angle kept in the particle (+0x50, which for some descriptions follows the particle's velocity, by an arctangent). The effect's box (+0x1A0) is widened to hold the particle.

`renderer.effects.block_b` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_second.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22), with the x87's own instructions only around FSIN, FCOS and FPATAN: their results have 64 bits of mantissa whatever the precision control, and the game multiplies some of them as they are, so those are kept as the FPU's ten bytes and each product rounded as the x87 rounds it. Read closer: the matrix a facing kind gets (0x0080B910, 0x0080B530, 0x0080B830 from the velocity made one long) and the others' turn are each multiplied out against one with the float at 0x00A81054 (zero) off the diagonal, as the game's code does, so a signed zero comes out as the game's. For a description whose +0x198 is above zero the particle's angle (+0x50) goes towards the matrix's own by the part over +0x198 times the float at 0x00AA0BF4 (a full turn added or taken off first when they are more than half a turn apart; set to it when it was -999) and the matrix turns by the difference; for one whose +0x19C is not zero it is tilted by the particle's +0x4C, which loses +0x1A0 times the step of itself and gains the step times the velocity against the effect's +0x13C to +0x144 (or, for flag 0x80, the description's +0xEC times the effect's +0x1B8 to +0x1C0), times +0x19C. Then the spin by the list at +0xC4, 0x005AC070 on the place (for a list at +0x108 and no bit 8 of +8), and the second block's matrix times the spun one (when its byte +0xC5 is set). The block's first byte goes up by one; its second is 5, 2 more for a scale that is not one, with +0xC5's bits. It only does arithmetic, so the comparison runs ours, keeps the effect, the particle, the place and the block it left, puts all four back and runs the game's. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, the four of the second class compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 780,628, 551,098 and 603,979 blocks. The test cities reach only the facing kind 4 (Big City), the other kinds' turn by the particle's angle, which is zero there, no spin, and the second block; so the turn towards the matrix's own, the tilt, 0x005AC070 and kinds 2 and 3 were run by a probe kept out of the code: on copies of real blocks' effect, description, particle, place and block with those switched on (and +0x1A0 at 0.1), ours and the game's, 0 differences in 111,926 probes but for the sign or payload of a NaN, which only the probe's made-up values gave (C++ on SSE does not keep the x87's rules for which NaN comes out). A mistake put in on purpose in the second block's product was reported (205,794 of 442,985 blocks); three that only change a rounding (the spin's cosine as a float, the angle's sine and cosine by the C library, the velocity divided by its length) went unseen, the test cities' angles and spin being zero.

**Five small functions.** `renderer.effects.small` replaces five functions of an effect that only read or write a few of its fields, written by hand: the box copied out for the manager's draw (0x005B26F0, six floats from +0x1A0), three floats from +0x34 copied out (0x005B2B00), the word at +0x1E8 set (0x005B2220, slot 0x34 of both classes), the count at +0x204 of the object an effect holds raised (0x005AF940), and the sub-object set going again (0x005B9CF0, slot 0x18: unless it is going and not told to go on, or marked to end when empty, the two marks are cleared and the byte +5 set). Each is compared by running ours and the game's on the same bytes. 2026-10-11, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (the box 1,600,745, 2,231,560 and 1,543,615 calls; the others between 2 and 9,378 a city, the place copied in two cities only). With the twenty-seven replacements of this section on, Big City's screenshots with clouds off match both runs without them in 20 of 20, and the camera workout ran to its end.

**An effect's transform.** Slot 0xC of both effect classes (0x005B3430 to 0x005B3817, no calls) takes two blocks of two bytes and thirteen floats, the kind the second class makes for its particles. The first is kept at +0x90 and the second at +0xC8, each with a count of the times it was set (+0x8C, +0xC4) and the block's second byte (+0x8D, +0xC5); an effect without +0x21A whose description has flag 0x1000 takes no place from the first block. Then the effect's own values that go by the transform are worked out again, up to its box (+0x194 to +0x1B4).

`renderer.effects.transform` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_setup.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22), compared by running ours and the game's on the same effect. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all nine of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 760,069, 484,665 and 938,019 calls. A mistake in the centre's place was reported.

**An effect started.** Slot 0 of both effect classes (0x005B3000 to 0x005B3423, with one word) starts an effect. It does nothing when the word is set for a description with a count at +0x18 and no flag 8, or when the sub-object cannot be set going again (0x005B9CF0). Otherwise the effect's clocks (+0x110, +0x120) and counts are set back and its pull (+0x13C) is the description's (+0xE0); descriptions with flag 0x800000 get a random delay and speed and the wander run once; the kind of the cell under the effect is asked of the terrain (0x00B43CEC, as for a new particle); and the emitter's values at +0x188 to +0x190 and the byte +0x118 are set, some from random numbers of the generator at 0x00B22DF8.

`renderer.effects.begin` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_setup.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). It writes the effect and draws random numbers, so the comparison runs ours, keeps the effect and the generator it left, puts both back and runs the game's. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all nine of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 4,335, 3,805 and 2,529 starts. Mistakes in the drift (flag 0x800000) and in +0x118 were reported; one in the direction from the side of the cell (flag 0x2000000) went unseen, no description in the test cities having it.

**An effect's render state by kind.** When an effect takes a description, 0x005B2260 fills its packed render state (+0x1C) with `SC4RenderState::Init` from one of eight rows of fourteen values, by the description's kind, 0 to 7 (a jump table at 0x005B2370); other kinds do nothing. The rows differ in a few values only: kinds 3 and 4 set the second value and a float of 0.9 or 0.5, kinds 2 and 6 clear the third and have -1 for the eighth, kinds 5 and 6 have 1 for the tenth where the others have 5 (kind 7 has 2, with 0 for the ninth).

`renderer.effects.kind_state` replaces it, written by hand with the eight rows as the game pushes them, compared by running ours and the game's on the same effect. 2026-10-11, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences, and only kinds 0, 3 and 5 occur (0: 10,030, 3,019 and 2,167 calls; 3: 409, 330 and 160; 5: 2,911, 3,804 and 3,092). The rows of kinds 1, 2, 4, 6 and 7 are copied from the code and have not run. With the thirty replacements of this section on, Big City's screenshots with clouds off match the two runs without them in 19 and 20 of 20 (the miss the smoke at zoom 1, 1.41% against 1.2%, the known noisy shot), and the camera workout ran to its end.

**An effect taking a description.** Slot 0x28 of the base class (0x005B7C90 to 0x005B8010) gives an effect its description (the object at +0x24 that says what kind of effect it is). A new description is held and the old one let go (0x005AF940, 0x005B1C80; the old one is destroyed when nothing else holds it). Then, new or not: the byte +0x21A is cleared, the render state is set by the description's kind, its texture is loaded (0x005B74C0 with the description's +0xD0, into +0x68), the batch at +0x28 is set up (slot 0x40, 0x005B7170), and the effect's values that come from the description are set: the rate of its clock (+0x130), four scales back to 1, the box cleared, the limits of where particles start (+0x200 to +0x214; with the terrain's height added for descriptions with flag 0x1000, or through 0x005B7030 for flag 0x2000), and whether the description has a pull of its own (+0x15C, +0x160). For descriptions with flag 0x100 the effects manager is asked whether there are effects named after this one with "_terrain" and "_water" (+0x218, +0x219: the two a particle starts when it meets the ground or the water).

`renderer.effects.description` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_setup.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). Four of the functions it calls cannot run twice (the hold and the let-go of descriptions, the counted texture, the batch's set-up, which keeps memory), so its comparison is the store's (`StSpansCompared`): the game's runs with those four noted, the hold and the let-go by their own detours, the texture (0x005B74C0) and the batch's set-up (0x005B7170, slot 0x40 of both classes) by detours in compare mode, each with the bytes it leaves in the effect (+0x68 and +0x6C; +0x28 to +0x67); the effect is put back and ours runs with the four answered from the notes. The render state (0x005B2260), 0x005B7030, the terrain's height and the names asked of the manager run for real in both. The calls and the effect must be the same. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all nine of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 12,671, 5,189 and 4,009 calls. A mistake in the scales was reported; ones in the pull (+0x18C), the second name asked about and 0x005B7030 went unseen: no description in the test cities has a pull or flag 0x2000 with 0x1000 (0x005B7030 itself has its own switch and forced comparison since, `renderer.effects.start_edge`), and the two names' answers are the same there. The second class's own version (0x005AEC90) is `renderer.effects.description_b`, below.

**An effect stopped outright.** 0x005B9CC0, on the sub-object: for an effect that is in use (+4) the two marks are cleared, it is told to end (slot 0x20 with two zeros, which gives every particle back and empties the list) and +4 is cleared. Letting go of what an effect holds (0x005B2440) ends in a jump to it. `renderer.effects.stop` replaces it, written by hand and compared by record and replay as the effect's draw. 2026-10-11, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (in use: 1,122, 511 and 351 calls; not in use, where it does nothing: 1,097, 428 and 315). With the thirty-two replacements of this section on, the camera workout ran to its end and Big City's screenshots with clouds off match as below.

**Letting go of what an effect holds.** Slot 0x14 of the base class's sub-object (0x005B2440): the object at +0x1EC is released (its slot 8), the description at +0x20 let go (0x005B1C80) and the object at +0x1E8 released, each field cleared first; then the effect is stopped outright. An earlier count had this as never called; it is, about two thousand times in a Big City run. `renderer.effects.letgo` replaces it, written by hand, compared by record and replay as the effect's draw, with the two releases seen as well: for the game's run each of the two objects is given a copy of its table of functions whose slot 8 notes the release, gives the object its own table back and passes the release on, and ours in the replay notes which objects it would release. 2026-10-11, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences in 2,221, 907 and 668 calls, of which 1,123, 495 and 352 released something. The second class's version (0x005AC140), for an effect in use, first releases its model instance (the sub-object's +0x21C) and its model (+0x220) through the part of the model that counts its holders; in the test cities those two are the same pointer, one object released twice, and the stand-in table stays until the second. It is ours in the same replacement, compared the same way (its two fields are past the bytes the record and replay keeps, so they are put back for ours' run by hand): 0 divergences in 25, 83 and 36 calls on effects in use (19, 60 and 14 with something to release) and as many on effects not in use. With the thirty-three replacements of this section on, Big City's screenshots with clouds off match the two later runs without them in 20 of 20, and the camera workout ran to its end. (One screenshot run of that night differed in sixteen shots by a tooltip: the mouse pointer was over the speed button. Run again, 20 of 20.)

**The second class taking a description.** Its slot 0x28 (0x005AEC90 to 0x005AEF1D) does nothing for a description without bit 0 of its third word of flags. Otherwise, after the base class's: the model instance (+0x220) and the model (+0x224) are released and the object at +0x278 ended (its slot 0x20), the part at +0x228 is cleared (0x008038D0 with 0), and a model is loaded (slot 0x54, 0x005AC610) by the description's +0xD0, or by one picked at random from the description's list at +0x1E0 when it has one. With a model: for descriptions with flag 1 the instance is given a block of values with one bit more set (its slot 0x40), and for flags 0x100 or 4 of the third word the part is set up on the instance (the part given the description, 0x005AC110; its +8 set to 10; the instance attached, 0x008038D0, the byte +0x26C saying whether that worked; and placed, 0x00802DB0 with (0, 0, -1)). Last the effects manager is asked for effects named after this one with two more suffixes (+0x26D, +0x26E).

`renderer.effects.description_b` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_model.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). Its comparison is the store's (`StSpansCompared` on the effect's 0x27C bytes and the generator at 0x00B22DF8), the game's run first with its calls noted, then ours with each answered from the notes. Noted are: the base class's whole run (0x005B7C90, by its detour, with the bytes it leaves in the effect's first 0x220); the releases of the instance and of the model's counting part and the end of the object at +0x278, through copies of their tables (the tail's proxies, slots 8 and 0x20); the load (slot 0x54, by its detour, with +0x220 and +0x274), after which the instance is proxied for its tint (slot 0x40, the block noted by its bytes but the two the game leaves unset); and the part's three functions (0x008038D0, 0x00802DB0 with its three floats, 0x005AC110), by detours in compare mode, each with the part's 0x44 bytes. Ours makes these calls through helpers that answer from the notes in the replay without touching the objects, which the game's run may have let go of. The pick among the description's models is made by ours from the same generator, which must be left the same; the tint block, the part's +8 (0x00989A60) and the names asked of the manager run for real in both. When the game's run let go of the model for good (its bytes changed), ours, which reads the counting part from the model, is not run and the call is only counted (`effects.description_b.skipped`). 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour; with `renderer.effects.model_b` and E1a's nine compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 167, 149 and 263 calls, 19, 11 and 54 more only counted. Reached there: the instance and the model released, a model by the description's ID and by a pick from its list, the part set up for flag 0x100 and for flag 4, attached and not. Never reached: the end of the object at +0x278, the tint (flag 1), and descriptions without bit 0. Mistakes put in on purpose were reported: the place's third float, +0x26F set where it is cleared, the pick one model off (reported in all three cities); one in the tint went unseen, as no description there has flag 1. With every `renderer.effects.*` replacement ours: parity 12 of 12 (muted, 7 days, with `determinism.dispatch_info`); screenshots with clouds off, Rush Hour 20 of 20 against each of two runs without them, Big City 20 of 20 against the second, while the first differed from both the second and ours in the same three zoom-1 shots (the smoke).

**The second class loading its model.** Slot 0x54 (0x005AC610 to 0x005AC86A, with the model's instance ID; nothing for 0) asks the resource manager (0x00B43CA8, slot 0xC) for the resource of type 0x5AD0E817, group 0xBADB57F1 and that instance. With it: the old model (+0x224) is released and a new one made from the resource (its slot 0x10), whose part that counts its holders is kept at +0x220 (the old one released); the model's slot 0x40 is called with 3; the largest side of the model's box (the instance's slot 0x1C) is kept at +0x274; and each of the model's materials (the interface 0x26A68448: a count at slot 0xC, each at slot 0x18) has bit 8 of its flags set or cleared by flag 0x400 of the description's third word and other bits by the description's kind (a jump table at 0x005AC870; kinds 5 and 6 also set two bytes).

`renderer.effects.model_b` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_model.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). The comparison runs both for real, on an effect that has no model yet (as its one caller leaves it; any other call is only counted, `effects.model_b.skipped`): ours first, whose model's materials are read (eight bytes each, where the flags are) and whose model is then released twice, as the class lets go of one; the effect is put back and the game's runs. The effect but for the two pointers, whether there is a model, and the materials must be the same. Its one caller is the second class taking a description, so with both compared the load runs one level down in that comparison's noted call. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on: 0 divergences in 108, 82 and 239 loads, all on effects without a model and all finding the resource. Reached: a counting part that is not the one held, the largest side the first or the second, kind 3 without flag 0x400. Never reached: an effect that already has a model, a missing resource, flag 0x400 and kinds 0, 2, 5 and 6 (the jump table's rows are copied from the code and have not run). Mistakes put in on purpose were reported: the third side taken always, flag 0x400 read the wrong way round.

**An effect's texture.** Taking a description loads the effect's texture (0x005B74C0, with the description's +0xD0): the handle at +0x68 is cleared; the framework (0x008793EC) is asked for the service 0x1AC0E11A by its interface 0xFAC0E219; with it, what the effect held at +0x6C is let go (0x008090E0), the service is asked for the texture by its ID into +0x6C (its slot 0x14), and on success +0x68 becomes the word the holder's first word points at. An effect takes its description again and again, so most loads are of an effect that already holds its texture.

`renderer.effects.texture` replaces it, written by hand, and compared by running both for real. For an effect that holds no texture yet: ours, whose texture is let go again as the function itself lets an old one go, then the game's. For one that holds a texture, where the old one can be let go only once: the game's, then ours on top of what it left. The handle, whether a texture is held and the rest of the effect must be the same. 2026-10-12, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (holding a texture already: 11,889, 6,149 and 5,363 loads; without one: 1,510, 737 and 475, of which 412, 326 and 160 found no texture). With the thirty-six replacements of this section on, the camera workout ran to its end and Big City's screenshots with clouds off match two runs without them in 20 of 20.

**The second class given a particle back.** Slot 0x40 of its sub-object (0x005AF390 to 0x005AF930). An effect with an object at its +0x278 whose particle's age (+4) is not below its life (+8) starts the particle again instead (0x005AF060). Otherwise the particle's own effect is stopped (its slot 0x10 with the effect's +0x18C) and released; an effect with its byte +0x26E set and nothing at +0x18C has the effects manager make the effect named after it with "_death" where the particle was (placed with a block built from the particle and the effect's matrix, its slot 0x34 called when the byte +0x26F is set, started with 0 and let go); and the particle goes back on the class's free list (0x00B0A5CC). Last, an effect with the object at +0x278, no particle left and its byte +0x10 set tells that object (its slot 4 with 0).

`renderer.effects.giveback_b` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_second.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). Read closer: the restart is for an age at or above the life (a NaN gives the particle back); the own effect is read again after it is stopped, and once more after the "_death" effect is made, when it is let go without +0x44 being cleared; the "_death" effect's block, for an effect whose second block has its byte +0xC5 set, is the particle taken through the second block and that block's matrix multiplied out against one with the float at 0x00A81054 off the diagonal (its second byte +0xC5 with bit 0), else the particle's place with a matrix of one on the diagonal and second byte 1. Its comparison is the notes' engine's, as the holders', the game's first: the effects manager, each particle's own effect, the effect the manager makes and the object at the effect's +0x278 get copies of their tables of functions whose slots note each call (on the effect's thread, outside other notes), and the game's runs with the calls passed on; the effect, the class's free list, the generator and the particles are put back and ours runs with every such call answered from the notes; the calls and those bytes must be the same, and what the game's left is put back; the restart is noted by a detour of its own (compare mode only). 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, the four of the second class compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 605, 2,844 and 160 give backs, each stopping and letting go the particle's own effect when it had one. Not reached: the restart, the "_death" effect and the object at +0x278. A mistake put in on purpose, +0x44 left as it was before the let-go, was reported (a second let-go).

**Two setters of what an effect holds.** The effects manager gives an effect the object it holds at +0x1EC through 0x005B26B0, and the second class's part is given its description (the part's +0x40) through 0x005AC110. Both do nothing when given what is held already; otherwise the new one is held first (the object's slot 4; the description through 0x005AF940), kept, and the old one let go (the object's slot 8; 0x005B1C80). `renderer.effects.holders` replaces the two, written by hand, compared with the notes' engine, the game's first: the holding and the letting go are noted (the object's through copies of the two objects' tables of functions, the description's through the detours those two functions have), the field is put back and ours is replayed. 2026-10-12, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences (the object: 1,098, 412 and 317 calls; the part's description: 3 calls a city). With the thirty-eight replacements of this section on, the camera workout ran to its end and Big City's screenshots with clouds off match two runs without them in 20 of 20.

**Making an effect.** The base class's constructor (0x005B2B50 to 0x005B2FC4) constructs the sub-object at +4 (0x005B9F00: its table of functions, the bytes and words up to +0x1C cleared, and an empty list at +0x10, a head node of 12 bytes that points at itself) and the batch at +0x28 (0x005EC690), sets the two tables of functions (0x00AA0EA0, 0x00AA0E58) and gives the effect's fields their first values: the two transforms as the unit one, the clocks' rates, scales of 1, the box. It leaves some of the object's bytes as it found them. `renderer.effects.ctor` replaces the constructor, written by hand in `src/core/effects_setup.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22; only the final value of a field the game writes twice is written), with the sub-object's and the second class's. They are compared on the object's own memory: ours runs, what it left is kept, its list's head is freed, the memory is put back as it was and the game's runs; the bytes must be the same but for the pointer to the head, and ours' head must have been an empty list. 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all nine of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 1,430, 695 and 593 effects made (the sub-object's twice each, once inside ours' run of the other); a mistake in a field was reported.

**The second class's constructor and the destructors.** The second class's constructor (0x005AC510) calls the base class's, sets its own two tables of functions (0x00AA0C58, 0x00AA0C10), clears the model's two pointers (+0x220, +0x224), constructs the part at +0x228 (0x00803060) and clears the words and bytes from +0x268 to +0x278; it is in `renderer.effects.ctor` now, by hand, compared like the other two over the object's 0x27C bytes. An effect's destructor (0x005A62B0, which a third class's destructor also ends in) releases the objects at +0x1F0 and +0x1EC, lets go of the texture at +0x6C (0x008090E0) and the description at +0x24 (0x005B1C80), and destroys the batch at +0x28 (0x005EC6E0) and the sub-object at +4 (0x005B9F40, which frees the particle list). The second class's (0x005AC5B0) first ends the object at +0x278 (slot 0x20), lets go of the description at +0x268, destroys the part at +0x228 (0x008030A0) and releases the model (+0x224, through the part of it that counts its holders) and the instance (+0x220), then goes on into the base class's. Each has a wrapper in the tables of functions (0x005B2FE0, 0x005AC590) that runs it and frees the memory when bit 0 of its argument is set. `renderer.effects.dtor` replaces the four, by hand. A destructor cannot be run twice, so the comparison is of the calls: the game's runs first with each call noted (the objects through copies of their tables of functions, the three destructors it calls and the description's letting go through detours that only this comparison puts in), then ours runs on the effect's bytes as they were, making no call but noting each one, and the two lists and the bytes left must be the same. The texture's letting go is in another thread's code and has no detour of ours: the texture is given one more holder for the game's run, must have lost exactly one, and is then let go for real. The two wrappers are three lines each and are ours in compare mode too, with no comparison of their own. 2026-10-12, three test cities with the camera workout: 0 divergences in 144 of the second class's constructor, 1,825 destructors of the base class and 144 of the second, and 1,825 textures. With the forty replacements of this section on, the camera workout ran to its end and Big City's other nineteen screenshots with clouds off match two runs without them (the smoke shot as below).

**A description's lists and its letting go.** The descriptions are read from the effects file when the game starts, 1,192 of them. Two of a description's values are lists, held as the game's own containers hold them (begin, end, end of the memory): one of records of seven floats (0x1C bytes), one of records of eight (0x20). Each has a reader ((stream, list), 0x005B0930 and 0x005B09D0) that reads the count (the stream's slot 0x24), resizes the list to it (0x005B0840, 0x005B08A0: cut short, or the game's insert, 0x005B05A0 and 0x005B0700, adding records at the end) and reads every float of every record (slot 0x30). `renderer.effects.lists` replaces the two readers and the two resizes, by hand; the inserts were still the game's then. The comparison lets the game's read the list, with the stream given a copy of its table of functions whose two slots note each read and the four bytes it gave; ours then reads into a copy of the list as it was before, the stream's copy handing back the noted values, and the reads, the two lists' lengths, their room and their bytes must be the same. `renderer.effects.release` replaces the letting go of a description (0x005B1C80: the count of holders at +0x204 one less, and at none the description destroyed, 0x0059ECB0, and freed), compared by running both on the count while a holder is left; the last letting go is the game's in compare mode and ours only when the replacement is on alone. 2026-10-12, three test cities with the camera workout (the file is the same in all three): 0 divergences in 1,192 reads of each list a run, 24 of the first and 33 of the second with records, and in 21,888 lettings go with a holder left. With the forty-two replacements of this section on, the camera workout ran to its end and Big City's other nineteen screenshots with clouds off match two runs without them (the smoke shot as below).

**A description's constructor and the lists' inserts.** A description is 0x208 bytes; its whole constructor (0x005B1660) first calls an inner one (0x005B0FE0) that gives the fields their first values, makes an empty string (eight bytes of the game's memory) and puts one value in each of seven lists through the game's growing inserts (0x00676CC0, 0x0050C3B0). `renderer.effects.description_ctor` replaces the inner one, written by hand in `src/core/effects_setup.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22). It is compared on the object's own memory: ours runs, what it left is kept, the memory is put back and the game's runs; the two must be the same field by field, a list's or string's three pointers by its length, room and bytes (`SuDescriptionsDiffer`; +0xD6, +0xD7, +0x1C8 and the count of holders, which it does not write, left out), and ours' lists and string are then freed. The inserts under the two lists' resizes (0x005B05A0, 0x005B0700: so many copies of a record put in at a place) are in `renderer.effects.lists` now, by hand as one function: with room in the list the records after the place move up and the copies go in, without it the game's growing insert (0x0059BBA0, 0x0059B570) does the whole job. They are compared by running ours on a copy of the list and the game's on the list. 2026-10-12, three test cities with the camera workout (the file is the same in all three): 0 divergences in 4,768 inserts a run. The rewritten inner constructor, 2026-10-23, the same three: 0 divergences in 4,770 descriptions constructed a run; a mistake in a field was reported. Every insert a run makes either adds nothing or finds no room and goes to the game's growing insert, so ours' moving up of records has not run. With the forty-three replacements of this section on, the camera workout ran to its end and Big City's other nineteen screenshots with clouds off match two runs without them (the smoke shot as below).

**A description's third list and the whole constructor.** The third list holds records of a string and a float (0x10 bytes; the string is the game's own, three pointers). Its reader (0x005B1D60) reads the count, resizes the list (0x005B1CD0) and reads each record's string (0x005811F0: the stream's slot 0x3C into a string the game keeps for it, then assigned to the record) and float. The resize makes a record with an empty string, then cuts the list short (0x005B1790, which frees the strings) or inserts copies of the record at the end (0x005B1530), which without room is the game's growing insert (0x005A17F0) and with room copies the record, moves the later records up and assigns the copies, all with the game's helpers for records that hold strings. The whole description's constructor (0x005B1660) is the inner one and then the count of holders (+0x204) cleared. All four are in `renderer.effects.lists` now, by hand. The reader is compared like the other two, with the stream's string slot noted as well: the game's reads the list, ours reads the same into an empty list of its own (every list is empty before its read), and the two must hold the same records, string by string; the resize and the insert are ours in ours' read and the game's in the game's. The constructor's own line is compared by running it on a count set to something else after the game's. 2026-10-12, three test cities with the camera workout (the file is the same in all three): 0 divergences in 1,192 reads of the third list a run, 6 of them with records, and 1,193 whole constructors a run. As with the other two lists, every insert goes to the game's growing insert, so ours' moving up of records has not run, nor has the cutting short. With the forty-three replacements of this section on, the camera workout ran to its end and Big City's other nineteen screenshots with clouds off match two runs without them (the smoke shot as below).

**A description read from the effects file.** One function reads a whole description from a stream (0x005B1DC0 to 0x005B21D5, (stream, description)): its numbers one after another (bytes, 16-bit and 32-bit words and floats, the stream's slots 0x14, 0x1C, 0x24 and 0x30), its lists through their readers (the three above at +0x108, +0x1B8 and +0x1CC, and the game's own for lists of floats, of points at +0x94 and of IDs at +0x1E0) and the string at +0x160. `renderer.effects.description_read` replaces it, written by hand in `src/core/effects_setup.h` (2026-10-23; a translation before, removed 2026-10-22); the lists' readers it calls are the ones above or the game's. For the comparison a description of our own is made with the game's constructor and the game's description is checked to be as new as that one (it always is); the game's then reads its description, with every read of the stream noted through the copy of the stream's table of functions (now every slot that reads a number, and the string's), ours reads the same values into ours, and the two descriptions must be the same as the constructor's are compared, the third list record by record (its string and value); ours is then destroyed (0x0059ECB0) and freed. A description that is not new is counted, not compared (`effects.description_read.not_new`; none has been). 2026-10-23, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (Big City, Making Money, Rush Hour, all nine of this piece compared in the same runs): 0 divergences in 2,384 descriptions read a run (the file is read twice); two reads put in the wrong order on purpose were reported.

**The compound descriptions read from the effects file.** Besides the 1,192 descriptions above, the start reads 1,154 objects of a second kind, 0x38 bytes each, which this section calls compound descriptions (what the game calls them is not known): a word of nine bits, a word, a list of records of 0x6C bytes (+8), a list of records of 0x18 bytes (+0x14), a string (+0x2C) and three words (+0x20). A 0x6C record is a string followed by numbers; a 0x18 record a word of four bits, a string, a float and a word. The reader is 0x005ABF50, with the two lists' readers (0x005ABE30 and, for one record, 0x005AB690; 0x005ABEC0), the lists' resizes (0x005ABCE0, 0x005ABD50, 0x005ABDC0) and inserts (0x005AB970, 0x005ABAD0) under it. Three smaller objects have readers of the same kind: a word and a string (0x005AB760, read twice), a list of points, a list of floats and a float (0x005AB860, 25 times), and a list of floats and four floats (0x005AB2F0, once). `renderer.effects.compound_read` replaces the twelve, by hand; the inserts are the same shape as the named records' above (without room the game's growing inserts 0x0059EDC0 and 0x005A07B0; with room the game's helpers for these records). The comparison is the description's with no constructor to make a new object with: ours reads into a copy of the object as it was, a list of plain numbers copied, a list of records required to be empty and without memory and a string empty (they always are), and what the two hold afterwards is compared field by field, strings by their bytes; four bytes of a 0x6C record that nothing writes (+0x16, +0x17, +0x52, +0x53) are left out. 2026-10-12, three test cities with the camera workout (the file is the same in all three): 0 divergences in 1,154 compound descriptions and the 28 smaller objects a run. As for the other lists, no list is cut short and no insert finds room, so those paths of ours have not run. With the forty-five replacements of this section on, the camera workout ran to its end and Big City's other nineteen screenshots with clouds off match two runs without them (the smoke shot as below).

**The clean-up after the draw** (0x005922A0 to 0x00592A0D; offsets here are from the manager object, 4 below the interface). The manager keeps six lists the same way: a table of pointers, a vector of the indices in use and a vector of the free ones. The first (table +0xB94, in use +0xBAC, free +0xBB8, and a fourth vector at +0xBA0 of indices waiting to start) holds what this section calls compound effects, objects that own 0x44-byte records (a vector at +0x114), each record holding an effect at +0x38. The clean-up clears the count at +0xC94 and sets the byte at +0x1B4, then asks each compound effect in use whether it has ended (0x005BFD70, which answers false while one of its records' effects still runs, and otherwise ends the compound effect itself). Those that have not are counted at +0xC94. One that has, and whose word at +8 is 1, is emptied: its records erased, its string at +0xC cut to nothing, +0x18 and +0x1C cleared, +0x20 set to -1, and its index moved from the ones in use to the free ones. Nothing is destroyed. Only in a frame where a compound effect was emptied are the five other lists gone through: the three lists of effects the draw walks (tables +0xBE8, +0xC30, +0xC54), a fourth (+0xC0C) and the decals (+0xBC4). An entry that only the manager still holds (the count its sub-object at +4 answers through slot 0x38, slot 0x10 for the fourth list, the word at +4 for a decal, is 1) and that is not running (its slot 8) has its index moved to the free ones; a vector with no room grows by its size, as the game's own do. Last the byte at +0x1B4 is cleared and the waiting effects are started (0x005921B0): the indices at +0xBA0 are added to those in use (the game's range insert, 0x00414040), the waiting vector is emptied, and each new one is started (0x005919D0, below, with the table's entry and 1); a start can leave more waiting, so this repeats, ten rounds at most, and does nothing while +0x1B4 is set. The erase (0x0058FEC0, (first, last) on the vector of records) copies the records after `last` down (0x0058E120, which assigns each through 0x0058DBA0), ends what the records past the new end hold (slot 0x20 of the effect at +0x38) and sets the end.

`renderer.effects.cleanup` replaces the four (0x005922A0, 0x005921B0, 0x0058FEC0, 0x0058E120), written by hand in `src/core/effects_cleanup.h`. The clean-up cannot run twice, since an emptied compound effect lets go of effects and the other lists go by who still holds what. Compare mode runs the game's first, noting each end test's answer and each erase with what the call changed in any compound effect, and stops it at its last call. There ours runs on a copy of the manager with copies of the twelve vectors, the compound effects put back as they were, the end tests answered and the changes made from the notes; the other lists' questions are asked again for real, being plain reads. The calls, the twelve vectors (what they hold and their room), the count and the compound effects' first 0x120 bytes are compared. The start of the waiting effects is compared the same way round (each start noted with what it left waiting), the erase as the setters above (the effects it ends seen through copies of their tables of functions), the copy by its answer. 2026-10-13, three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on: 0 divergences in 78,558 (Making Money), 70,432 (Big City) and 88,310 clean-ups (Rush Hour), in 79,651, 78,320 and 89,748 calls of the start, and in 13,816, 40,269 and 20,370 erases. An earlier Big City run counted what they did: 5,137 compound effects emptied, 15,792 effects of the other lists freed and 9,754 started in 32,768 clean-ups. Three faults put into ours on purpose (a wrong word written, a wrong test in a list, a start left out) were all reported. Not run by any test city: an erase short of the vector's end, so the copy never copies a record and its loop is unchecked; the range insert finding no room is the game's code either way.

**The sort by depth.** The draw sorts its vector of {depth, effect} with six functions, an introsort as the game's library has it. The loop (0x005906C0) works on a range while it has more than sixteen entries: it takes the median of the first, middle and last depth, partitions the range about it (0x0058F090), calls itself on the upper part and goes on with the lower; its depth limit, twice the binary logarithm of the count, sends a range to a heap sort (0x0058F9D0) that no test reaches. The ranges of sixteen or fewer are then put in order by insertion: the first sixteen entries with a sort that checks for the front (0x0058F980, each entry through 0x0058F4B0, which puts a value below the first entry at the front and passes any other to 0x0058D900), the rest with one that trusts an entry before it not to be above (0x0058E170). All compare two depths as "below", false for equal depths. `renderer.effects.sort` replaces the six, written by hand in `src/core/effects_sort.h`; compare mode runs the game's and then ours from the same vector, and the game's calls of the ones under it are compared on the way. 2026-10-13, the same three runs: 0 divergences in 57,645, 71,979 and 104,419 calls of the loop, 36,214, 40,071 and 25,249 partitions, 21,431, 31,908 and 79,170 plus 12,368, 12,381 and 10,193 insertion sorts, and 247,184, 233,689 and 426,287 plus 226,746, 212,136 and 368,358 inserts. The comparison finds the vector through the manager, which it first sees at a clean-up, so sorts before the first clean-up of a run are not compared (logged once per function; how many was not counted).

**The start of a compound effect** (0x005919D0 to 0x005921AC, (compound effect, first) on the manager object). A compound effect's description is one of the compound descriptions above (the manager's table at +0xAC, the compound effect's index into it at +0x20), and each of its 0x6C-byte parts names one effect and the levels of detail it runs between (the bytes +0x4C and +0x4D, against the manager's level at +0xC84; the level before is at +0xC88). A record of the compound effect is one part running: a count of its changes (+0), a byte from the part (+1), thirteen floats (+4: a matrix, a place, a scale), the effect (+0x38), which part (+0x3C). The start first goes through the records there are. One whose part is no longer within the level has its effect stopped (slot 4 with 1) and let go (slot 0x20); the others are kept, moved down over the gaps (the record's assign, 0x0058DBA0), and told the level's values again when the level has changed; the records past the kept ones are erased. Then it goes through the description's parts that have an effect (+0x68): those new to the level (within it now, not before), or all of them when `first` is set. Parts of one group (the word +0x64) are alternatives: one draw below 0x10000 from the manager's generator (+0x1C0) is made for the group, the parts' weights (+0x66) are added up, and the part with which the sum reaches the draw is taken. A part taken gets a record at the end of the vector (in place when there is room, else through the vector's grow, 0x005905C0), its effect made (0x00591730, below), the level's values, the part's thirteen floats, and the effect its transform (slot 0xC): the compound effect's own floats (+0xE0), the part's, or, when both the part's byte +0x15 and the compound effect's +0xDD are set, the two put together (the part's place turned by the compound effect's matrix, scaled and added to its place; the matrices and the scales multiplied). The effect of a compound effect that runs (+0x24 set, +0x25 clear) is started (slot 0). Last the compound effect's own values go to every record's effect (0x005BFCD0). With the manager's byte +0xB8C set every record is stopped, the description is looked up again by name or IDs (0x0058F5A0, 0x0058F2E0) and every part is taken; no test city sets it.

The four small functions under it: a record's constructor (0x0058E3E0: the matrix one, the scale 1, no effect) and its copy constructor (0x0058E020, which holds the effect once more, slot 0x1C); the level's values (0x0058DC50: two floats, each going from the part's first, +0x54 and +0x5C, to its second, +0x58 and +0x60, by how far the level is into the part's levels, an integer division, told to the effect through its slot 0x14 as the two values of 0x102); and the compound effect's own values (0x005BFCD0: for each record and each kind of value in the vector at +0x12C, the effect's slot 0x14 with the kind, the values in the table at +0x138 and their count).

`renderer.effects.compound` replaces the five, written by hand in `src/core/effects_compound.h`. The start makes effects, so it cannot run twice. Compare mode runs the game's first and notes each call it makes that ours makes too: the level's values, the assign, the erase, the grow, the making of the effect and the compound effect's values through detours, and the stop, the letting go, the transform and the start of a record's effect through a copy of the effect's table of functions (given to the effects the records hold before the run, and to each new one as it is made). With each note goes what the call changed in the compound effect and its records, the bytes only that call changed. Then ours runs on a copy of the compound effect as it was, with a copy of its records and the generator set back; its calls are only noted, each followed by the changes the game's call at the same place made. The calls with their arguments (the transform's block and the record given to the grow among them), the compound effect, its records and the generator's state are compared. The four small functions are compared wherever the game calls them: the constructor by running both on the same bytes, the copy with the hold counted on both sides, and the two that tell values by ours first with the effect's slot 0x14 only noting, then the game's. On the three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (2026-10-13): Making Money 15,982 starts, Big City 47,913 and Rush Hour 24,239, with 0 divergences, and none in the small functions (the level's values 16,107, 52,188 and 22,365 calls; the constructor 10,382, 38,493 and 14,121; the copy 10,821, 39,011 and 14,764; the compound effect's values 6,567, 16,208 and 8,717). Three mistakes put in on purpose were each reported: the start's argument turned round (5,397 of 47,574 starts on Big City), a float of the part's own transform changed (21,370 of 47,084) and the scale of the two put together (4,984 of 22,503 on Rush Hour), so all three kinds of transform run.

**The making of a record's effect** (0x00591730 to 0x005919A4, (part, record, a word the start keeps from one part to the next) on the manager object; the start is its only caller). What a part's record holds need not be an effect of particles: the part's word at +0xC says what it is and its index at +0x68 which one. Kind 0 is an effect of particles, as many as the part's count (+0x4E; the record ends up holding the last). Its description is the manager's (the table of pointers at +0xCC), and the description picks the class: the first class for one without flag 1 (the byte +8), the second for one with it whose float at +0x1D8 is 0, a third otherwise. The new effect takes the description (slot 0x28), and each after the first is told one value of 0x101, the part's word at +0x50 times its place in the count (slot 0x14). An effect of the second class of a part with flag 2 (+0x10) is remembered in the start's word, after the one remembered before has been given it (0x005AC1C0, which keeps it at +0x278) and it has been marked (0x005B2230, the byte +0x21A that stops its emitting); any other effect clears the word. The record then holds the effect (slot 0x1C) and lets go of what it held (slot 0x20). Kind 1 is a decal, given its description (0x00583480; 0x64 bytes each in the manager's table at +0x10C). Kind 2 is another compound effect: it is given the part's name, the name is looked up among the compound descriptions (0x0058F5A0), the index is kept at its +0x20 and the description given to it when there is one (0x005BFF80), and the record holds its part at +4. Kind 0x10 is an object given a word of the manager's table at +0xEC (slot 0x3C). The kinds 3 to 0xF each have a list of the manager's (0x20 bytes each from the pointer at +0x158, the vector at +0x14): the entry at the index is asked for an object (its slot 4), the record holds it and gets flag 2 at +0x40; an index outside the list leaves the record alone. The decal, the compound effect and the asked object are given to the record through one small function (0x0058D8D0: the new one held, the old one let go, nothing for the same one). Last the record's flag 4 is set or cleared as the part's flag 1 is. Each thing is made by a maker of its own that takes a free one of the manager's list or allocates one: 0x00590BC0, 0x00590D40 and 0x00590EA0 for the three classes of effect, 0x00591160 the decal, 0x00590A60 the compound effect, 0x00591000 the object of kind 0x10.

`renderer.effects.make` replaces the two (0x00591730 and 0x0058D8D0), written by hand in `src/core/effects_make.h`. The six makers, the decal's and the compound effect's descriptions, the look-up of a name and the two calls for a remembered effect stay the game's. The making cannot run twice either, so compare mode is the start's kind: the game's first, with those functions noted through detours and the new effect's taking of its description, its value and its hold through a copy of its table of functions, given to it as its maker returns; the entry a part of the kinds 3 to 0xF asks is watched the same way. Then ours runs on a copy of the record with its calls only noted and each maker answered with what the game's made. A compound effect it is answered with is a copy, so that the index ours stores in it is compared too, and the name ours gives it is checked against the name the game's left there (the string's assign is every string's and is not noted). The calls, the record, the start's word and that copy are compared, pointers into the record and to what was made by their place. The small function is compared wherever the game calls it, the game's with the hold and the letting go noted, then ours from the same pointer with them only noted. It shares the making's one detour with the start's comparison, and the two comparisons run together. On the three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (2026-10-13): Making Money 9,089 makings, Big City 37,632 and Rush Hour 13,589, with 0 divergences, and none in 3,276, 22,721 and 5,226 calls of the small function. Of what Big City's first 32,768 makings made, 13,286 were effects of the first class, 465 of the second, 10,198 decals and 2,986 compound effects, and 5,833 were asked of the lists; Making Money asked none in its first 8,192 and Rush Hour 52. No test city makes an effect of the third class or an object of kind 0x10, and none reaches the two calls for a remembered effect (a coverage run; those lines of ours have not run). Five mistakes put in on purpose were each reported on Big City: the record's flag 4 as 8 (6,926 of 37,295 makings), the compound effect's index stored at +0x24 (3,427 of 37,059), the asked object's flag 2 as 1 (6,638 of 36,185), the wrong flag of the description picking the class (478 of 34,059) and the small function's test turned round (all 18,943 of its calls). With the start's comparison on as well: 36,621 makings and 47,065 starts, 0 divergences.

**The makers** (0x00590BC0, 0x00590D40, 0x00591160 and 0x00590A60, each with no argument but the manager object). Each kind the manager makes has a list of three vectors: a table of pointers to every one it has made, which it holds, the indices in use and the free ones (effects of the first class: the table at +0xBE8, in use +0xBF4, free +0xC00; the second class +0xC30, +0xC3C, +0xC48; decals +0xBC4, +0xBD0, +0xBDC; compound effects +0xB94, free +0xBB8). A maker with a free one takes the last free index, puts it among those in use and answers the one at that index; a compound effect's index goes among those waiting to start (+0xBA0) instead. Otherwise it allocates a new one (operator new, 0x005E55E0) and constructs it. An effect has its sub-object's slot 0x10 called and is given the manager's block at +0xCA0 (slot 0x34); one of the first class is also given the manager's object at +0xDEC (0x005B26B0) when there is one and the options' byte +0x33C is set. A decal is reset (0x00581F80: the byte +8 cleared and, when the byte +0x75 is set, its thirteen floats from +0x78 made the identity's, +0x75 cleared and the count at +0x74 one more; the word +0x70 set to -1). A compound effect holds the manager at +0x148 (the manager's slot 4). The new one's index, the table's size, goes among those in use (or waiting), and the new one goes into the table, held (slot 0x1C, a compound effect's slot 4), in place or through the game's insert (0x0058FF70, a compound effect's 0x00430A90). A hold taken around that is let go after. Only the decal's and the compound effect's makers test for a failed allocation.

`renderer.effects.makers` replaces the four and the decal's reset, written by hand in `src/core/effects_makers.h`. The constructors (0x00583190 and 0x005C0150 the game's; the effects' two are `renderer.effects.ctor`'s) and the inserts stay as they are. A making that takes a free one only moves an index between two vectors, so compare mode runs the game's and ours on the same memory: the manager's lists and the storage of the two vectors the maker adds to, put back as they were between the two. One that makes a new one cannot run twice. The game's runs first with its calls noted: the allocation in the pool allocator under operator new (0x005B9FC0; operator new's own entry is `determinism.zero_alloc`'s), the constructor, the taking of the manager's object and the reset through detours, the calls on the new one, its sub-object and the manager through copies of their tables of functions. Then ours is replayed on the lists put back, each call answered as the game's was. The calls, the lists, the answer and the manager a new compound effect holds at +0x148 are compared. The reset is compared by running both on the decal's bytes. A making that would grow a vector (about forty in each city's run) is not compared. The detours of the makers, the effects' constructors and the taking of the manager's object are shared with the making's, the constructors' and the holders' replacements, and all five comparisons run together. That combined run found that the stand-in engine of the earlier comparisons gave every object back its own table when one of its comparisons ended, which took the making's and the makers' stand-ins off new effects mid-run (1,087 false divergences); it now gives back only its own. On the three test cities with the camera workout, clouds and water effects on (2026-10-14), 0 divergences: Making Money 8,888 makings that took a free one, 611 that made a new one and 106 resets; Big City 38,692, 1,837 and 288; Rush Hour 14,848, 961 and 124.
